- 129 -

Issued June 10, 1929

AUSTRIAN PATENT OFFICE

PATENT SPECIFICATION No. 113487

Viktor Schauberger in Purkersdorf (Lower Austria)
Installation for torrent control and river regulation

Filed January 31, 1927 — Patent duration begins:
January 15, 1929

The invention relates to an installation for torrent control and river regulation, by which the velocity of the water is braked to such an extent that the sediment carried along cannot cause dangerous destruction, and further to influence the movement of the water through brake installations at appropriate points in such a way that the theoretical stream line is pushed toward the middle of the current.

In the drawing, the subject matter of the invention is schematically illustrated; Fig. D shows an exemplary embodiment of the water brake device in the form of brake weirs installed transversely to the river course.

The brake weirs D are suitably made of reinforced concrete and anchored in the ground by extensions 4, so that they cannot be displaced by the oncoming water. Facing the direction of the water course, the weirs have a hollow groove, onto which the water runs and is thereby flung upward, whereby it loses the greater part of its energy and is no longer able to move larger rock pieces.

The brake weirs are installed in the stream course at larger or smaller intervals from each other, depending on the gradient. To shift the theoretical stream line toward the middle of the river in the further course, water brakes are installed — according to the invention — on the side of the scouring and bank undercutting, perpendicular to the respective river direction, as shown in Fig. A. In this figure, the brakes are designated 1, while the opposite points illustrate the deposition of sediment. The stream line shifted by the

- 130 -

installation is indicated by the center line.

Fig. B shows the installation at a larger scale, and Fig. C the cross-section through such an installation.

The installations 1 (in Fig. B) are essentially triangular in shape, are drawn up toward the bank, and gradually slope toward the stream center (installation instructions: see sketch).

The effect of these installations is particularly evident from Fig. C, in which the solid line represents the cross-section of the riverbed before installation, and the dotted line after prolonged action of the installation.

Between the installations 1, the carried sediment is deposited, and a zone of dead water forms near the bank, which serves as a guide for the flowing water masses and protects the bank from undercutting (Fig. B).

The fully drawn line 2 designates the stream line before installation, while the dotted line designated 3 shows the stream line under the effect of the installation (Fig. B).

PATENT CLAIMS:


---


1. Installation for torrent control and river regulation, characterized in that it is formed with a hollow-groove shape on the side of the incoming water, so that the impinging water is thrown upward and backward, or toward the channel center.

2. Installation according to claim 1, characterized in that the cross-section standing perpendicular to the current has a triangular shape.

- 131 -

Issued August 25, 1933

AUSTRIAN PATENT OFFICE

PATENT SPECIFICATION No. 134543

Viktor Schauberger in Vienna
Water conduction in pipes and channels.

Filed August 12, 1931 — Patent duration begins:
April 15, 1933

The subject of the invention is a water conduction system which aims to increase the through-flow volume of water compared to smooth water conduits, channels, pipelines, and the like.

According to the inventor's view underlying the invention, turbulence phenomena in conventional water conduits are partly caused by temperature differences in the various water layers, but mainly by the fact that the water masses sliding along the walls have substantially different velocities than those near the center, whereby rolling processes occur at the transition layer.

To prevent sedimentation, it is known to install in the water conduit — projecting from the wall toward the center — guide-vane-like curved surfaces, each curved in such a way that they push the water from the wall toward the center of the water conduit. It is also known to provide the inner wall of pipes with helical raised portions in order to impart a circulating movement to the water.

The invention now relates to a further development of these measures for the purpose stated at the outset.
In the drawing, the subject matter of the invention is shown in exemplary embodiments. Fig. 1 shows a perspective view into the pipe, Fig. 2 a single guide vane seen more from above and front against the flow direction, and in Fig. 3 seen in the pipe circumferential direction. Fig. 4 shows a channel according to the

- 132 -

invention. Fig. 5 illustrates a cross-section through a guide vane provided with twist-like grooves running in the direction of the water flow.

In the pipeline 1, the guide-vane-like surface groups 2, 2', 2'' are installed. Each group of surfaces 2, 2' and 2'' lies in the course of a twist 3, or 3' and 3'' respectively, as indicated by the dotted helical lines.

The guide surfaces themselves are curved plowshare-like in the sense of the twist and rise in the flow direction so that they push the water toward the pipe center and impart a rotation about the pipe axis to it.


---


In Figs. 2 and 3, which show a single guide surface seen more from front and above, and from the side respectively, the dashed arrow indicates the flow direction in the smooth pipe, while the solid arrow 5 represents the course of the current threads displaced by the guide surface.

Analogous guide surfaces can also be installed in channels. In this case, the individual guide vanes naturally do not stand in a twist, but rather straight one behind the other, and are arranged symmetrically on both sides of the vertical median plane, as shown in Fig. 4.

The vane according to Fig. 5 is provided on its guide surface with twist-like grooves 6, through which the movement of the water in the course of a twist movement is also supported in the vertical direction. Pipes equipped with vanes designed in this way are particularly suitable for the transport of goods in heavy water, e.g., ores and the like.

PATENT CLAIMS:

1. Water conduction in pipes and channels with built-in guide-vane-like surfaces projecting from the wall toward the center, each curved so as to push the water from the wall toward the center of the water conduit, characterized in that the guide-vane-like surfaces are arranged in pipes in the course of a multi-start twist, and in channels symmetrically on both sides of the vertical median plane of the channel, one behind the other.

2. Water conduction in pipes and channels according to claim 1, characterized in that the guide-vane-like surfaces deflecting the water from the wall toward the center are provided with twist-like grooves in the flow direction of the

- 133 -

water.

Issued January 10, 1934

AUSTRIAN PATENT OFFICE

PATENT SPECIFICATION No. 136214

Viktor Schauberger in Purkersdorf near Vienna.

Installation and facilities for regulating the outflow channel of reservoirs and increasing the strength of their closure dam.

Filed April 23, 1930. — Patent duration begins: August 15, 1933.

The invention relates to an installation with associated facilities for regulating the outflow channel of reservoirs and increasing the strength of their closure dam, and consists in particular in that, depending on the outside temperature, a selectable mixture of heavy and light water from the reservoir is automatically fed to the outflow channel, wherein the heavy water to be fed to the outflow channel is conveniently used for cooling the valley-side wall layer of the closure dam of the reservoir, by being sprinkled with heavy water.


---


It was recognized that in all hydraulic engineering measures, an important circumstance was neglected for the management of the water channel, namely the temperature of the water in relation to the earth and air temperature, as well as the temperature differences in the flowing water itself, and further recognized that the temperature differences existing in the water and constantly changing decisively influence the movement of the water. If, through artificial structures such as dam systems or the like, the natural channel is divided and the outflow of the reservoir takes place either only through bottom gates (which discharge only heavy water at a temperature of about +4°C) or only through dam-crest overflow (whereby only light water at the respective maximum water temperature is fed to the channel), disturbances occur in the channel, which particularly in channel bends cause destruction of the banks. If, however, water corresponding to the respective outside temperature — i.e., correctly tempered — is fed to a channel, then depending on the desired outcome, either a braking of the water masses and reduction of their tractive force, or conversely an acceleration of the water masses and

- 134 -

increase of their tractive force, can be achieved. Thus, instead of only locally acting bank-protection structures, a disturbance-free outflow of the departing water masses can be achieved merely through the regulation of correct water temperatures — i.e., through the automatic establishment of a permanent state of equilibrium in the water itself. Widening of the channel through sediment deposition or its ejection (gravel banks) and bank erosion, particularly in curves, can be avoided through special dam installations with appropriately built-in provisions, and incorrect outflow conditions can be re-regulated. Through special adjustment of the control devices for light and heavy water provided in these dam installations, the temperature gradient corresponding to the respective outside temperature can be re-established, whereby, in particular, the flood hazards can be almost entirely eliminated.


---


Simultaneously with the regulation of the outflow channel, the facility required for this — namely the closure dam of the reservoir of special construction — can also have its strength increased, in that the pores of the masonry are sealed by cooling the water particles penetrating from the reservoir side, thereby eliminating the cause of destruction of the masonry. The light water, whose temperature is reduced and which penetrates the masonry pores, loses its carrying and dissolving capacity for salts and other substances, until it reaches that state at the temperature of +4°C at which its dissolving capacity is lowest and the filtering effect of the wall is strongest. If therefore the light water penetrating into the wall is cooled on its way through the pores of the wall, by sprinkling the valley side of the dam wall with heavy water at +4°C, the water penetrating into the pores precipitates its dissolved substances in the pores, thereby sealing them, so that after a few weeks a dense pore-seal of the reservoir wall is achieved, which makes any further safeguard against destruction of the masonry superfluous. If, however, the described cooling of the valley side of the masonry is omitted, the water penetrating from the reservoir side into the pores — particularly when the dam wall is exposed to sunlight — is warmed from the valley side and gains dissolving capacity with respect to the solid component of the building material. The pores are leached out. The destructive effect increases with increasing pore enlargement during frost; wall cracks arise,

- 135 -

into which new water quantities enter both through the hydrostatic pressure and through the flow pressure of the water, until, with the help of frost, complete destruction of the masonry occurs, particularly at the level of the normal water surface.

In the drawing, an exemplary embodiment of a facility for the installation — namely an abutment wall of a reservoir — is shown in Fig. 1 in section and in Fig. 2 in plan, while Fig. 3 shows a detail, concerning the control device for the bottom outlet, in section.

For the purpose of regulating the outflow quantities of cold heavy water and warm light water, bottom outlets 0 are provided in the abutment chamber K of the reservoir B — as shown — on both sides thereof, whose gates T are controlled by a temperature-controlled float G. The pipelines II of the bottom outlets 0 lead up to the dam crest K1 of the closure dam, wherein bypass pipes U1, U2, and U3 — which can be closed by gate valves I1, I3 as required — branch off from the riser pipe II at various heights and are led to the valley side of the dam K, where they open into corresponding troughs of the dam wall. At the foot of the valley-side wall face, a installation K3 is conveniently built in for vortex formation and better mixing of the water masses flowing over the wall.


---


The gates T of the bottom outlets 0 sit with a cutting edge on a threshold sunk into the channel bed and are guided, sealed on relieved rollers in vertical grooves, and connected by means of a guide rod F, which runs in a shaft of the lateral dam wall H, to the float G, which is designed, for example, as a diving bell. In the lateral dam wall H, at various heights above the bottom outlet 0, there are tubular passages A, which enable communication of the shaft in which the float G is submerged with the free reservoir water surface. By filling the riser pipe W with the gate T open, communication between the riser pipe and the reservoir is also achieved, which relieves the closure gates T from one-sided water pressure and thereby enables their尽可能 friction-free height control. The gate can therefore, particularly as a wooden construction, be precisely matched to the carrying capacity of the diving bell G, so that its easy movability is guaranteed at every water level. The diving bell G, which is mounted on its guide rod F so as to be vertically displaceable, can thus

- 136 -

be adjusted to any desired floating height through the respective height setting. The bell possesses in its cover a closable air valve P, through whose opening the air compressed inside the bell can escape, whereby the gate T is immediately closed. Through a pipe R, open at both ends and provided with a height scale, the depth of penetration of the water into the bell can be changed at any time by raising or lowering it. With the diving bell fully lowered without an air cushion, compressed air can also be introduced through the described pipe to raise the float, which — with valve P closed — enables opening of the gates T. The air cushion enclosed in the bell during normal operation is thus in direct communication with the atmosphere through the bell wall, so that — particularly with metallic bell walls — the outside temperature is imposed on the air cushion, thereby influencing its volume. Depending on the volume increase or decrease of the air cushion in the diving bell G, a different height control of the bottom gates T is achieved depending on the outside temperature, so that the quantity of heavy water fed to the channel — which is brought to the valley side of the dam through the bottom outlets 0 and the riser pipe II as well as one of the bypass pipes U1, U2, or U3 — is varied depending on the outside temperature. The light water flows over a suitable overflow plate above the dam crest of the reservoir into the channel.


---


The mixing between heavy and light water is facilitated both by the installation K3 at the foot of the valley side of the dam wall and by the fact that the heavy water is fed transversely to the vertically falling light water in the horizontal bypass pipes U1, U2, U3, whereby an intimate mixing is achieved through the artificially arising vortex formations. When the individual diving bells G are exposed to sunlight, the associated bottom gates T are raised further, and thereby a larger percentage of heavy water is fed to the channel in relation to the light water flowing over the dam crest, whereas at cool outside temperatures the bottom gates are fully or nearly fully closed, and only warm overflow water is fed to the channel.

The heavy water led to the dam crest for better mixing can simultaneously be used to increase the strength of the closure dam. After the dam wall K, initially — for example through the bypass pipe U2 — heavy water is fed solely to the lower part of the dam K on its valley side, for which purpose the diving bell G is set so that the bottom gates T remain permanently open, whereby any overflow over the dam crest is entirely eliminated and the inflowing water is fed exclusively to the channel through the bottom gates 0. This heavy water sprinkling the valley side of the dam wall now cools the wall from outside in such a way that the light water of the reservoir penetrating from the mountain side into the wall pores deposits its dissolved substances in the wall and seals it. After sealing the lower wall part, heavy water can then be fed through the bypass pipe U3 to the dam crest, whereby the upper part of the dam wall can also be sealed. Depending on the quality of the masonry, this sealing process takes several weeks, after which the pores of the masonry are clogged and any further risk, even in normal operation, is no longer to be feared. After sealing the masonry, the overflow plate M — which may be merely an iron structure placed on the dam crest — can also be removed, in order to produce, instead of the descent of light water over the plate M shown in Fig. 1, a sprinkling of the dam crest and thereby a protection of the dam masonry on the valley side.

PATENT CLAIMS:

1. Installation for regulating the outflow channel of reservoirs and increasing the strength of their closure dam, characterized in that facilities are provided through which, in dependence on the outside temperature, a selectable mixture of heavy and light water from the reservoir is automatically fed to the outflow channel.

2. Installation according to claim 1, characterized in that the closure dam (K) of the reservoir is provided with facilities through which its valley side is sprinkled with heavy water for cooling.


---


3. Facility for the installation according to claim 1, with controllable bottom outlet of the reservoir, characterized in that the control of the bottom outlet (T) is effected by a temperature-controlled float (G).

4. Facility for the installation according to claims 2 and 3, characterized in that the heavy water of the bottom outlet (T) is led through pipelines (W) to the dam crest (K1).

5. Facility according to claim 4, characterized in that the heavy water is fed to the valley side of the dam (K) in horizontal

- 138 -

cross-troughs (U1, U2, U3) at various heights above the foundation.

6. Facility according to claim 3, characterized in that the float (G) is designed as a vertically displaceable bell with variable air volume.

7. Facility according to claim 6, characterized by a displaceable communication pipe (R) in the bell cover communicating with the atmosphere.

8. Facility according to claim 5, characterized in that the individual bypass pipes (U1, U2, U3) for the heavy water pipeline are connected to the common riser pipe (W) through individual closable shut-off organs (V1, V2).

- 139 -

LETTERS to WALTER SCHAUBERGER (t20)

Vienna-Hadersdorf, October 8, 1941

Dear Walter!

Yesterday a telegram arrived from Reichenberg announcing that an exact report on the state of things there would arrive this week. I suspect that I will soon travel there.

In order to support your propaganda factually, the following:

Today's river regulation is well known for straightening river courses in order to steepen the geological gradient by shortening the development path. This commits a bottomless stupidity, and for this reason:

The geological gradient is the weight-based driving force, in order to allow the water to swing freely in organic curve generators.

These curve generators have a dual purpose:
a) to mechanically swing out the water in order to separate substances that react to centrifugal force (the carbonic-acid values) from substances that — like all atmospheric waste substances (fertilizing oxygen values) — do not react to centrifugal force, i.e., centripetalize.
b) to excite the swung-out water masses, which are influenced oligodynamically by sliding resistance, and in this state to guide them toward the longitudinal axis, where the most highly excited fruit substances (geospheric origin type), the fertilization substances moving in the longitudinal axis, which become cool and thereby inactive through relaxation (separation of the two bi-polar basic substances), are to be bound.


---


Thus the organic analysis and the organic synthesis come about, the latter conditioning the construction of higher carrier substances (juvenile water) and the construction of juvenile carrying and hauling forces (inner life-energies of amorphous kind), which rejuvenate the inverted old water and upgrade it by one octave.

Just as in the dynamo the soft-iron core forms the armature, in the river generator the oxygen-saturated longitudinal axis forms the "armature" around which opposite basic substances orbit in spiral paths, which in the most highly excited state then circle the reversely charged longitudinal-axis substance concentration, and then bind these fertilization substances. With this, the high-quality synthesis product is

- 140 -

ready, which breaks through the bank walls at the ford points (see green discharge direction), radiates horizontally, after which it then, through vertically incident ultra-rays of the sun, comes to new combinations and thus to the construction of groundwater. (see overleaf sketch;)

With this you are in a position, as a low-current technician, to give appropriate information about nature-near river regulation.

Best regards, your
Father

Dear Walter! October 15, 1941

In addition to yesterday's letter, you can tell Dr. Carl that I will put the most ruined river course in order, also rivers that have too small or too large a geological gradient.

Every river must be treated individually; while in general the natural scheme f applies to all, each river must nevertheless be examined for which illness it suffers from.

You may accept any proposal with him, however, I reserve myself against any intention of fraud. For in this field I have experienced all sorts of things.

Nature-near river regulation is so simple that a single example suffices to remove the scales from the nature-disconnected eyes of even today's water expert.

I would have to decline being treated only for the instigator, and then, once the people see what it is all about, receiving a kick.

I need no engineers for my regulation work, no project, but only some good stonemasons and woodworkers, who can be found anywhere.

In my rivers there is no flood damage, no bank erosion, no undercutting, and almost no need for subsequent repair. The most tired rivers become fresh and living, cool and clean, and the wildest rivers tame. Today's torrent control is also absolutely nature-incorrect and therefore wrong. So negotiate on whatever

- 141 -

basis with Dr. Carl. As long as I am not cheated again, any clause is agreeable to me that stays within the framework I wrote to you yesterday. For even death is not to be had for free, and therefore nothing unreasonable will be demanded, which is also not the case with me.

Best regards, your

Father

- 142 -

CONTROVERSY OVER THE RHINE REGULATION (t21)

Bregenz, November 15, 1935

To the Government of the Principality of Liechtenstein in Vaduz

With best thanks, we return the booklet No. 137, Tau, September 1935, kindly transmitted on November 6 of this year, after inspection.


---


From the available reports W.Z., it would have to be concluded that real evidence is verifiable for the presented ideas. Following this line of thought, Forchheimer — well-known as a researcher in the field of hydraulic engineering — facilitated the publication of Schauberger's articles in No. 20 of July 15, 1930, of the professional journal "Die Wasserwirtschaft" (Vienna III, Dampfschiffahrtstrasse 10), as they aroused his full interest and, through their completely new perspectives, appeared suited to act not only as a stimulating but even as a pioneering influence on river and dam construction.

The publications, which must first be reworked into a unified set of principles and clear formulation before serious study, have — as would be evident from various issues of "Die Wasserwirtschaft" — attracted interest.

Schauberger, however, did not really respond to factual inquiries. Ministerial Councilor Ing. Ehrenberger, head of the hydraulic engineering testing institute in Vienna, whose ability to assess hydraulic engineering questions is beyond any doubt, published in "Die Wasserwirtschaft" No. 9 of March 25, 1933, the results of tests he conducted to examine the fundamental bases of Schauberger's theories. He summarized the results as follows:

Both on the basis of laboratory experiments with differently tempered water at 10–20°C, and on the basis of control measurements on the flushing channel in Neuberg cited by H.S. himself (8.7°–16.7°C), a practically measurable influence of temperature or temperature gradients on flow velocity could not be determined. All conclusions based on this false premise, concerning the methods of hydraulic engineering in general and river engineering in

- 143 -

particular, are therefore invalid, and the sharp attacks by Schauberger in this direction must be decisively rejected.

In this publication, which preliminarily concludes the matter, Schauberger's articles are also cited: "Die Wasserwirtschaft" No. 20, 24 of 1930, and No. 3, 5, 9, and 10 of 1931.
A letter to the editorial office, which is reproduced in No. 10 of 1931 and is informative for assessing the details of Schauberger's reports, shows that Schauberger was not overly anxious about factual accuracy in his publication. Since we lack the means to carry out a review of claims even approximately as thoroughly as Min.-Rat Ing. Ehrenberger did, we must leave the examination of further details to scientific research.


---


We believe, however, that we may venture to state that Schaubergers theoretical treatises are to be placed in the same line as the gold-making of Schappeller in Aurolzmünster, Upper Austria. Another, whose name presently escapes us, filled the daily newspapers about sixteen years ago with a kind of science, claiming to be able to raise subterranean treasures—principally ores of every kind—by means of rays, or to generate mechanical energy, using comparisons such as that the generation of energy in thermal power plants occurs through a suitable combination of water, coal, and air.

Schappeller also sought and found financial backers for the further pursuit of his gold-making ideas.
We believe that a further pursuit of Schaubergers relentlessly critical proposals is unnecessary.
With the expression of the highest regard:

Rhine Construction Director Eng. Nesper

- 144 -

Schauberger's Reply to the Government of Liechtenstein

Vienna, 25 November 1935

To the Government of the Principality of Liechtenstein, Vaduz

Most Honored Head of Government!

Hofrat Dr.Pr. handed me yesterday two letters in which Herr Oberingenieur Böhi and Herr Eng. Nesper take a most peculiar stance on the statements of Werner Zimmermann (Tau Journal, September 1935).

Herr W.Z. described to me some time ago the catastrophe to be expected in the Rhine lands (St. Gallen Rhine Valley), which the daily newspapers are already discussing; similarly, former Chancellor Herr Dr. Ender described these dangers in a similar sense, if it should prove impossible to lower the Rhine by a few meters.
This lowering cannot and will not be achieved through bank corrections or dredging. Through such measures the dangers must multiply many times over, because thereby the tractive forces of the water—which is the sole decisive factor here—diminish.
Here only an organization of the necessary tractive forces can help.
The evidence for this is shown abundantly by the constant increase of danger through cutoffs, bank corrections, and so forth.

I had at the time already intended to explain the causes of this increase in danger, and was supported in this by the renowned hydrologist Dr. Forchheimer and the world-famous scholar Excellency Wilhelm Exner. Unfortunately, these explanations were suppressed by certain official authorities, and for this reason I could no longer publicly refute the wholly erroneous explanations given by Ministerial Councillor Eng. Ehrenberger. Nor was it possible for me to orally explain to Chancellor Dr. Dollfuß, who repeatedly sought me out personally in this matter, the errors contained in Ehrenberger's report, owing to his death.


---


You, most honored Head of Government, should not attribute any financial motives to the following explanations, but only purely human interest.
After such personal attacks I decline monetary advantages, and for this reason the following explanations serve only to rehabilitate two men whom I hold in high esteem and therefore cannot permit purely human endeavors to be answered

- 145 -

in such a manner, without being able to present substantive counterarguments.

Every movement, however it may be named, is accompanied by losses of energy when no refreshment or nourishment of the moving body occurs along its path.
Water that is conducted in a single-profile channel warms through direct solar influence or friction. Through these thermal effects there occurs a slow but steadily acting discharge of intra-atomic energies. The consequence of this intra-atomic discharge is a loss of motive force, which gradually increases and produces fatigue phenomena that cause sediment deposition and the rising of the riverbed.

To warm one cubic meter of water by only 0.1°C (such temperature differences can be found in the smallest cross-sections) requires an energy equivalent to 42,700 kg/m. 0.1°C = 42,700 kg/m, 1°C = 427,000 kg/m, 20°C = 8,540,000 kg/m or 114,000 HP. The Rhine carries approximately 500 cubic meters per second, therefore the total loss at a warming of 20°C = 57,000,000 HP or 42,500,000 kW. Every temperature increase of only 0.1°C therefore means a great energy loss that must be replaced if dangerous sediment deposition is to be avoided. Dams cannot help here, because the gradient will one day flatten out through constant sediment deposition and the water must break out sideways. And with that the flood catastrophe has arrived.

How then can one prevent this flattening of the gradient? Only by replacing these necessary energies that have been lost through the sun's rays.
If one considers the energy losses that occur when a river course warms by about 20°C, then it also becomes clear how necessary it is to methodically replace these losses of tractive force. Through mechanical acceleration (cutoffs, etc.) this loss can only be intensified through strong aeration, and for this reason other means must be employed here to maintain these tractive forces in flowing water. These means are hitherto unknown pulsation phenomena that trigger cold effects.

Rivers that cool along their course carry their sediment far out into the seas owing to the preservation of their kinetic energies (lagoon formation); rivers that warm along their course and lose these energies form deltas and block their mouths.
Where then lies the cause of this changeable behavior?
A great law has escaped science, by which nature

- 146 -


---


steers and guides the flowing waters.
Two kinds of dissolution processes take place in water:
a) A dissolution process that occurs under the influence of warmth and light.
b) A dissolution process that occurs under the exclusion of warmth and light.

The sediment particles carried in suspended form in the water are insoluble in case (a). These substances can only release their energies when certain ionization processes occur, which we know from the so-called photoelectric effect. Positive metals release negative electrons under warmth and light. With negative metals the process reverses under the influence of light exclusion and cold. These radiations are measurable and have a strength of approximately 2 volts when alternating-polarity metal groups are present.

We can find metals in any water. The specifically heaviest metals sink first owing to their weight and remain lying. The specifically lighter parent rock continues further.
And so it must come to a metal poverty in the lower course and therefore to fatigue phenomena (loss of tractive force), because without the occurrence of ionization phenomena no intra-atomic energies can be freed.

Freeing energies can only bind themselves in cooling water and under exclusion of light. If the water is warm in itself, then the energies (electrons) that likewise split off are lost in the atmosphere.

Every absorption process must be followed by after-reactions if internal charging of the water is to occur, but these can only reappear when the temperature gradient reaches a certain low level. These after-reactions are chemo-electric fusion phenomena that transform gases into electrozoic (animal) energy products. If this mutation cannot take place, then even the strongest ionizations are of no use. If the temperature gradient is correct, then the flowing water becomes an animal accumulator. Water that is too warm is acidic and too conductive, and for this reason an internal discharge then occurs that fatigues the substance itself.

Through dredging the evil is only magnified, because through the dredging holes the heaviest rock (the metal-bearing rock) sinks and is largely lost.
Through the intra-atomic radiations, magnetic forces arise that diminish the absolute weight of the sediment, and so it happens that such sediment behaves contrary to the Archimedean law—i.e., the heavier (more metal-bearing) it is, the more lightly it floats

- 147 -


---


My regulations, or rather organizations, of water have the consequence that stones or driftwood that are heavier than water float freely in the river axis, because there the greatest coolness prevails and the strongest mutation processes take place, which render the water gas-free, dense, and thereby mobile.
If the water, unimpeded in its movement, shoots forward midstream, then the normal or double profile forms automatically, as we can see in every natural watercourse. The blood capillaries, etc., are built according to this system, because without this double profile there could be no value phenomena, which are all of dual nature (negative and positive).

And here too, sketched in brief strokes, lies the secret of why in all civilized states the river courses are perishing and causing ever higher maintenance costs each year. Here too lies the secret of the decline of the forests (qualitative decay), the degradation of the soil in qualitative terms—in a word, the cause of the world crisis, which is merely a consequence of the disturbance of valence that the modern forester (clear-cutting), the modern water expert (bank regulation), the modern farmer (artificial fertilizer economy), and the modern energy expert (exploiter of coal, which is destined for formative movement and not for movement formation) cause in absolute ignorance of the simplest laws of nature.

With this sketchy explanation I hope to have enlightened you, most honored Head of Government, sufficiently that you understand why Hofrat Pr., who knows my researches most precisely, is doing everything to open the eyes of humanity. Herr Werner Zimmermann also belongs among those endowed with much natural feeling and has taken on the thankless task of working in an enlightening sense in Switzerland.

An adverse judgment is often easily formed.
The greater the danger, the more naked the truth must be; and this truth is always bitter, especially when official regulations themselves are the cause of the misery.

Errors committed against nature are always crimes, and for this reason the people who absolutely refuse to be instructed must atone for what they have committed against Mother Earth.
Whether done consciously or unconsciously plays no role here—it always comes down to the causes that then trigger the sad facts that we see, for example, in the Rhine today.

- 148 -

Deign, most honored Head of Government, to accept the assurance

of the highest regard

HUMOR PROOF (t22)

How I came to the University of Natural Resources and Life Sciences


---


I did not attend the University of Natural Resources and Life Sciences in Vienna in order to learn, but to instruct the assembled professorial college there on how to regulate rivers in accordance with nature. This came about as follows: the world-renowned hydrologist Professor Dr. Philip Forchheimer was commissioned by the then Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry in Vienna to prepare an expert opinion on the log flume facility designed and built by me in Neuberg, Styria, which was described in the official commissioning report as a technical wonder because on this facility woods that were specifically heavier than water (beech, larch, etc.) swam like fish, contrary to the Archimedean law. In addition, this facility achieved an equally inexplicable reduction in operating costs of approximately 90% compared to previous log driving costs. Professor Forchheimer studied the facility for about six weeks without getting to the bottom of why and how this facility contradicted the hitherto regarded as irrefutable laws. I had no reason to betray the secret, and so the professor, deeply shaken in his previous views, persuaded me to answer and respond before the assembled faculty at the University of Natural Resources and Life Sciences.

When I arrived at the said school, the janitor asked me whom I was looking for and what I wanted here. I stated the reason and purpose

- 149 -

of my visit and was directed with the words: "The gentlemen are expecting you anyway," to the first floor, door 7, where I found about ten professors, including well-known water experts, in the large lecture hall. Professor Forchheimer first introduced me to the rector and, with a sweeping gesture, to the other gentlemen, whereupon His Magnificence led me to the presidential chair standing at the head of the large table and, with a peculiar smile and corresponding gesture, invited me to sit down. The look, the manner of the rector, and the derisively smirking smiles of the other gentlemen, who ostentatiously seated themselves only after I had taken my seat, prompted me to respond to this strange reception as follows:

"So, Herr Wildmeister, you wish to instruct us, the experts, how in your opinion we should in future regulate watercourses in accordance with nature"—he stressed this word—"so that through this measure no damage to fields and waters arises, but, as you have published, only advantageous after-effects occur along the entire developmental line as a biological consequence of nature-correct regulation."
"Well," I answered after a brief pause of reflection, "the art of nature-correct watercourse regulation cannot unfortunately be explained so simply and in a few words."

"Perhaps it is possible," the rector interjected, "to make the core of the matter visible with a few catchwords. So please, as briefly and concisely as possible—how do or how should we regulate rivers!"


---


"So—like the boar pisses"—was my spoken answer, word for word, slow and suitably emphasized. The effect of this unexpected answer was as I expected and wanted it to be. Namely—a general wrinkling of noses, which His Magnificence, playing with his pencil, condescended to end after a general embarrassed pause as follows: "Herr Wildmeister—we would ask you to express yourself somewhat more elegantly and above all more objectively..." At that moment Professor Forchheimer rose and said: "Magnificence, gentlemen—I find this prompt answer not only hits the nail on the head, but is also substantively unassailable. Please follow me to the blackboard." Having arrived there, he covered the large blackboard from top to bottom with formulas that were incomprehensible to me. He got so into the swing of it that the chalk broke several times, which he threw aside in annoyance each time, which His Magnificence acknowledged with most displeased glances and nervous twitching—took another, a new one, continued painting, and finally, when the upper

- 150 -

blackboard became too small for him, he pulled up the one hanging below with a strong yank and also covered it more than halfway with his hieroglyphs. Then he stepped back a little and began to lecture. Naturally I understood not a word, but the others seemed to understand him well. For in the next moment a debate ensued that lasted at least two hours and broke off abruptly when the janitor appeared, who reminded His Magnificence of something, whereupon, speaking a few words of apology, he gave a hasty greeting, shook my hand particularly warmly, and said: "We must discuss this matter thoroughly once more."

With that my first and last stay at the University of Natural Resources and Life Sciences came to an end. Professor Forchheimer took me under his arm and invited me to accompany him. He forgot to take leave of his colleagues. I gave a brief greeting and went with my impatiently departing companion. At the gate, Forchheimer looked at his watch, was visibly startled by the advanced hour, and said: "You know what, come to my apartment tomorrow at 9 o'clock in the morning, and then we can discuss this case, which interests me highly, in all peace and quiet. If only you had given me this answer earlier, which is worth writing a textbook about." I agreed, he adjusted his bowler hat, I thanked him—and he was gone.

The next day I appeared in the Peter-Jordan-Straße—punctually. "Well now, let's talk quite precisely about the curve that you indicated yesterday. But first tell me how you came up with this particular comparison. It is indeed truly apt—this 'like the boar pisses.'" chuckled the small but inch-by-inch dignified scholar.


---


"So come on—where did you get this comparison from!" "Well," I answered, "this comparison does not originate from me, but from my late father, who explained to his woodsmen with these words the nature-correct laying of the log flume—so that the over-heavy woods would swim." Forchheimer looked at me questioningly, and so I began to explain to him the concept of "laying" (Verlegen) and its purpose.

Log driving in the narrow mountain brook was an art that required very particular knowledge. First, the water was scarce for the heavy wood, and second, the curves were tight. The only thing that helped was good "laying." This laying had the purpose of accelerating the swimming speed of the wood through braking curves. "Momenterl," the professor threw in—"so you mean one must brake the water in order to accelerate it,"


---


- 151 -

"not a bad idea, for then the water is compressed and gains more tractive force." "No, Herr Professor, that is not what was meant. These braking curves serve above all the purpose of turning the water spirally about its own axis, just as water turns before every drainage hole." Forchheimer noted something, waved his pencil stub and impatiently demanded that I continue speaking. "Have you, honored Herr Professor, ever observed a boar when it pisses?" "No," answered the scholar, and kept writing. "Well then, please imagine the oscillation curve that arises when the running boar pisses. It—that is the highly interesting thing—is that thereby the ideal cycloidal space curve, as beautifully as one could not possibly construct it, arises." And after these words the scholar attempted to draw this particular curve. He soon gave up, however, because one cannot draw this spiral curve, whose cross-section equals the elevation. "Well, I shall calculate it." I sat patiently and let the professor calculate. He scratched now behind his right ear, now behind his left, threw away one sheet of paper, took another, fetched back the discarded one, compared it with the later inscribed one, threw both away, took yet another, began to curse, and finally declared that to calculate this would take years—if the mathematics of today were even sufficient for it at all.

I answered: "Herr Professor, what we are dealing with here is the curve in which and through which—life—arises." From this answer there developed a long and broad philosophical conversation that revolved mainly around the undefinable concept of life. "I am," said Forchheimer finally, "a Jew and cannot go along with this. If you stand me on my head, only formulas fall out of me. You think within a framework whose boundaries neither you nor any human being knows. We shall not get ahead this way. For today it is enough. I already have a headache. Come back—I shall name the day and hour."

I agreed and left. On the way home I met the well-known writer and former frigate captain Deskovic, with whom I spoke about this case. A few days later Deskovic visited me and invited me to see Excellency Wilhelm Exner, whom my case interested burning-hotly and who wished to speak with me.

Exner received me very kindly and asked me: "Do you know the brake that holds water steady on a steep slope?" I answered—"yes, Excellency." He replied, "understand me correctly, I mean no mechanical—but an inner safety brake." My answer was—"I understand you exactly, Excellency, what you mean by this inner brake." Exner called an old spinster, had her bring him a cigar, which his secretary lit for him, and when it was burning, he said to me: "So—and now tell me precisely how you see this,


---


- 152 -

inner water brake that has been tormenting me for decades."
"Before I answer this question, I must first tell you, Excellency, how and as what I—see water." Exner smiled peculiarly, nodded kindly to me, and said briefly, "correct," and with a gesture invited me to continue speaking. "If," I continued, "water were that which the hydraulic engineers take water to be, namely a chemically dead substance, then there would long since have been no water and no life on this Earth. I regard water as the blood of the Earth, in which, although not the exactly same process as in our blood, nevertheless a similar process prevails, that enables its movement in our organism and thereby its self-governing—original—mode of movement. I would like to compare this inner mode of movement, the cause of the possibility of physical movement—since every other comparison is lacking to form a mental picture of something invisible—with an opening flower bud that, in its unfolding, builds a cup-shaped wreath of leaves, in whose center stands the flower stem and at whose tip the actual secret of movement, life in statu nascendi, in the form of a concentrated movement."

I made a small pause, in order to gather from some utterance of Exner's whether the scholar understood me in this circumlocutory flower-language, or whether it would go with me here as it usually did when I spoke of these things with an authoritative scientist and was generally dismissed as a fantasist, a fool, or otherwise as a chatterer not to be taken seriously. In Exner's eyes, however, I noticed something that prompted me to step one level higher into this profound subject matter. "Unfolding—Excellency—I regard as the biological consequence of a preceding concentration of energy-substance, and this in turn as the consequence of a radiation that I regard as the relatively highest, or in this case as an already highly organized expansion—taken together—the whole as a rhythmic process of oscillation caused by inner forces—for which the suitable word is lacking to represent it pictorially. Life itself, which springs, so to speak, as the last product of unfolding from this primal ground, is the highest conceivable concentration of energy-substance, which unfolds for the last time, through external environmental influences, for a fraction of a fraction of a second—and then, as a unique and singular apparent birth, extinguishes forever. What remains behind is a raw-material and physical fruit, as a consequence of the temporary flagging of the aforementioned inner out-swinging of that which is still too inferior for next-higher developmental processes, and solidifies in the falling light of the sun that produces concentrating heat forms."


---


- 153 -

These unfolding processes, which also take place in water through constant concentration processes—the water-flowers and the solidification—birth of young water that is birthed from this upward-flowing radiant chalice, are mediately the cause of the inner old-water brake. The faster the old water flows down the steep slope and thereby receives the varied impulse-shocks through resistances—stones, etc.—the stronger become the reactive recoil effects that brake the water, which is externally unbraked on the steep slope, through inner growth processes, by means of the discharge of the upward-unfolding water-flower chalices. The secret of this young-water birth, which takes place in reversed river direction, is the cycloidal space-curve movement of the thereby pulsating water. The means to the end, to build this concentrating spiral movement, is the trout, whose throat and gills possess the dispersing organs through which, after the splitting off of the relatively most inferior expansion products, the aforementioned energy concentration, its specific densification, and the aforementioned discharge—the building of the water-flower chalice—take place, in the midst of which the actual dynamide-substance precipitation occurs, which leaves behind the upward-drawing quality-substance-filled apparent void, whose function the organic vacuum is—the most effective suction force that exists on this planet.

The building of the corresponding counter-pressure, which also acts upstream and necessarily arises as a logical consequence of the upstream-unfolding aforementioned water-flower chalice, I described to the scholar similarly, as depicted in the preface of the Trout Motor. "Well," answered Exner after a short pause, "I can more or less understand what you are saying, but I advise you not to speak of it before third parties for the time being. Try to explain in simple words what you have just told me, write it down, put the written text in an envelope, which I shall seal before you—without reading the contents—and deposit at the Academy of Sciences, of which I am the chairman, for later times, so that your authorship of this discovery is preserved. I would not want anyone to steal this right from you, and I shall speak with Deskovic about it so that he may place a small note somewhere as a cover." I did so and never saw Exner again, for he died soon after.

A few days later Professor Forchheimer appeared at my place, highly agitated, tore his bowler hat from his head, set it upside down on the table-top so that the stiffener suffered a nasty crack, and


---


- 154 -

rounded on me: "How and why did you run to Exner, who understands nothing of these matters, and deliver a thoroughly confused lecture about you at the Academy of Sciences that only provoked laughter, deposited a letter from you, and thereby made himself and you ridiculous before the assembled college of professors!" I told Forchheimer how this came about, also mentioning Deskovic, whose name Forchheimer dismissed with a contemptuous gesture and said: "For these explanations I am the one who is here, and now you will write about it in the water management journal, but give me the manuscript beforehand for correction." Thus came about my publications "Temperature and Water Movement" in the Austrian Water Management Journal, which were later banned. "In a few days we are going to Professor Schoklitsch and Professor Smorcek in Brünn," he declared categorically and left.

Schoklitsch showed us his experimental laboratory and pointed with pride to glass plates over which his test water flowed, which prompted me to remark that I had never in nature seen water flow over such plates. Professor Schoklitsch took this interjection very amiss, and so I redirected the conversation to a turbine guide vane lying in a corner that showed particularly severe cavitation holes. From the answer that Schoklitsch gave to my question of how he explained these cavitation phenomena in the steel guide vane, I saw that the man had no understanding of decomposing dynamide-substance precipitates, which arise as a consequence of over-speed turbines that stand in a certain compass direction—see the relevant explanations in the book "German Physics"—a pressure peak of approximately 32,000 atmospheres according to measurements. These are precisely the reversed synthesis-substance products, which I subsequently discussed in detail with Professor Smorcek, head of the Technical University, whom we visited in his workshop. For one can call these test sites, which do not allow the inner movement processes of water to be considered, nothing else. During this visit the cause of the collapse of the dam was discussed, which had led to the condemnation of the builder, and it only emerged years later that here too it was a matter of cavitation phenomena similar to those that destroy the hardest turbine guide vanes, often within a short time. I drew Smorcek's attention to the different effects of building-up and breaking-down synthesis-product types, found great interest—without, however, coming into closer contact with this scholar either. Smorcek mentioned that he would come to Vienna in the near future and would like to go with me to Professor Schaffernak, head of the water testing department of the Technical University, to discuss these and other questions jointly. Forchheimer took a negative stance and declared that this visit would be pointless,


---


- 155 -

because Schaffernak was too much of a materialist and, for example, could not explain why the Danube and the Inn do not mix at their confluence—but only slowly, far downstream.

This visit did nevertheless take place, accompanied by Smorcek, and Schaffernak, who posed the aforementioned question—which seemed to be his pet subject—was given the explanation by me that the cause of this phenomenon lies in voltage and temperature differences that only permit water mixing in the lower course after these have equalized. Schaffernak looked at me peculiarly, asked how I came to this idea, and also posed other questions, from which I again recognized that this water expert too regarded the origin of all life as a chemically dead substance and its most finely divided sediment substances, in the highest developmental form, the bacteriophage boundary values, as impurities of the blood of the Earth, which in reality are the actual sources from which life arises in statu nascendi—the negatively charged germ-substance tensions—which, wedded with downstream-flowing, finely scattered and dosed (mixed) fertilization substances, produce the inner equalizations and their products that bring about the becoming-alive of the then pulsating water. A closer understanding was not possible with any of these scholars. Only Forchheimer said thoughtfully: "I am glad that I am already 75 years old. You can no longer harm me. But there will come a time when people will speak of before and after Schauberger—just as they speak of before and after Christ—about the differences in belief of then and now." In the course of the decades I had frequent occasion to speak with scholars of world renown about the concept of atomic smashing and atomic building, among them also Geheimrat Planck in Berlin, who only looked at me wide-eyed but did not say a word about my views. He eventually put me in touch with the chief chemist of the Kaiser Wilhelm Institute, whose conversational consequences led me, by a roundabout way, into the insane asylum and the Mauthausen concentration camp. This only by the by, to indicate the dangers that exist when one speaks of these things with people who feel that they are on a nature-incorrect path of knowledge, yet wish under all circumstances to preserve their existence.

The nature-correct river regulation will and can only be understood when the above-described inner movement processes have become common knowledge, for which perhaps still decades of development time will be necessary in order to recognize the tragic effects of today's purely mechanistic watercourse regulation methods through the very worst experiences

Concluding this chapter, one thing only can be said here: The present watercourse regulation, which not only takes no account of the essential—the inner enlivenment of the water—but on the contrary does everything to prevent the water's coming to life and to suppress the natural propagation and reproduction possibilities in such a way that one can truly wonder that there is any water and vegetation left at all.

Everything we see around us or can otherwise perceive has come into being through the cycloidal space-curve movement serving self-renewal (development). A type of movement that can only arise when the watercourse, thanks to braking resistances located on the bank, which convert the starting movement into a spiral-like orbital movement type, can develop. The cycloidal braking curves that the professor cited in his last textbook edition shortly before his death under my name. He had the intention to write a book about these braking curves that trigger the rhythmic oscillation process in naturally-conducted water, but died after the first outlines of this book were already finished. Forchheimer, as he expressed to me shortly before his death, had the intention with this book to refute everything that he had implanted in millions of minds in his textbooks as a quasi-leading guide. Namely, that one has to lead the water down from the mountain to the valley by the shortest and steepest—most straight-line—path, so that it can flow into the sea, if possible without being able to cause damage. Forchheimer saw shortly before his death that in doing so he committed the greatest teaching error of his life as a leading hydraulic engineer. Namely—the destruction of the natural development path, in which the starting movement crosses with the upward- and rebuilding orbital movement in rhythmic succession. For at the intersection point of this movement-change process, under quite specific other conditions, the upbuilding, the propagation- and reproduction-capable germinal substance for the birth of the physical firstborn, the blood of the Earth, is set free—nearly mono-polarly, and thereby so highly active that it is capable of consuming what can scatter stones or dose the gills of trout for the purpose of building up their own movement forces. And that which, in this life-renewal process, radiates away as the superfluous and expands upstream—that is what gives birth to the riddle of water, when, as a consequence of the inner expansion force (radiation force) of the germinal substance products becoming highly active through cooling influences, the fertilizing substance covering the water-flower calyx, the mechanical covering mantle arises, at whose

walls, finely distributed and well mixed (dosed) with the carrying medium, that which is violent is located, which ONLY in this spatially weakened (disassembled) and inwardly passive state can be consumed (bound) through the aforementioned cooling influences—that which the German poet-prince Wolfgang von Goethe called the Eternal-Feminine, the All-Attracting.

I have succeeded in decomposing various substances (elements and their compounds, minerals, metals, etc.) into their fundamental substances with the help of slight temperature differences and regrouping these. In this way, the possibility exists to generate arbitrary energies in and from water.

The practical exploitation possibility of this discovery cannot yet be surveyed at present, but undoubtedly means a complete transformation in all fields of science and technology.

I have already built larger installations, with co-use of the discovered lawfulness, in the field of log floating and river regulation, which, as is known, have functioned without complaint for a decade and still present unsolvable riddles to the respective professional groups today.

The measures currently customary in forestry, agriculture, water and energy management will, like many teaching and fundamental principles of physics,

- 159 -

chemistry, botany and geology, undergo principled alterations. Even medical science will not remain untouched by this discovery.

Furthermore, it is possible to regulate watercourses over arbitrary distances without bank structures, to guide wood and other substances, even when they are heavier than water, for example ores, stones, etc., centrally, to carry out groundwater table elevations country-wide and to supply to the groundwater those substances that are necessary for the respective vegetation.

Finally, it thereby succeeds in making wood and other substances incombustible and resistant to decay, in artificially producing drinking and healing waters for humans, animals and soil in arbitrary composition and mode of action, but on the same path as occurs in Nature, in conveying water vertically upward in pipelines without pumping devices, in producing electrical and radiation energies in arbitrary strength nearly cost-free, in raising soil values, in healing cancer, tuberculosis and nervous diseases.

Naturally, I am prepared within certain limits to grant authoritative persons insight through corresponding experiments, so that they can convince themselves of the correctness of what has been set forth.

I seek character-firm and generously thinking people who will help me to convey the many advantages of the discovered lawfulness to all humanity, without distinction of races and worldviews. The research results may, however, under no circumstances be exploited only for personal advantages, but should serve the welfare, peace and re-health of all humanity.

I call upon our leaders and our authorities to do everything so that clarity be created about the important questions unfolded below. May they not stand hostile toward a matter that is to bring salvation. The time is too serious and too heavy to be prolonged through stubborn resistance. Even if it is understandable that one does not gladly give up the god one has worshipped up to now.

The disturbed circuit—cause of the crisis


---


---
