In our time, the longing for Nature makes itself increasingly noticeable.

- 160 -

This urge toward Nature, which is strong, calm and healthy, is the necessary appearance of the present time, the counterweight to an anorganic civilization that we mistakenly designate as culture.

This civilization is a work of man, who, self-sovereignly, without regard for the real happening in Nature, has built up a senseless and foundationless world that now threatens to destroy him who should be its lord, because he has disturbed through his actions and his work the sense of unity prevailing in Nature.

If today we stand helplessly and counsel-lessly before our creations and must increasingly recognize that our so-called labor with all its worries serves only self-destruction, shows not a glimmer of improvement in any direction, and we drift hopelessly toward a dark future, then it also becomes understandable that more and more people are finding themselves who, disgusted by this senseless doing and driving, seek the paths that lead them back to the All-Mother Nature.

Man is a being created by Nature according to her laws and therefore dependent on her.

His work, the pseudo-culture created by him, became in the course of time a senseless and disconnected absurdity that, through the enormous power of technical aids, has become such a gigantic monstrosity that it nearly already reaches our natural forces, at least, however, already manages to intervene disturbingly in the great life-machinery of Nature.

Man, who in the great life of Nature yet represents only a little spark, a microorganism, has, stimulated by short-lived pseudo-successes, developed an activity that begins to disturb the great living-together and seems to prepare an end to the qualitative production of our macroorganism Earth.

If, therefore, an economic decay spreads all around man despite accumulation of quantities, and many branches of production show a retrograde movement in qualitative respect, yes, even decay-herds begin to form everywhere that now already seize man himself, if despite all searching no means can be found to protect man in living body from decay, then this is only the lawfully correct consequence of his own actions. Without knowing the great lawful regularities prevailing in Nature, he reached with senseless greed into the only life-generating organism Earth, which now begins to paralyze the blaspheming hand with elemental force that has dared to disturb the serving-the-whole working of Nature.

This lawfulness prevailing in Nature, unique in its greatness and unity, which manifests itself in every living creature and in every organism, is the law of the eternal circuit, which is bound in every organism to a definite time span and to a definite velocity.


---


- 161 -
If this cycle, in which every event stands under the influence of what came before, is accelerated or slowed in its tempo or altogether halted by some intervening force, it can no longer serve its lawful purpose, to which it is subject together with all things created by nature. The affected organism falls behind, drops out of the direction of the great stream, and all organisms bound to it for better or for worse fall prey to death, which ultimately also brings to decay the very hand that senselessly intervened and caused the whole catastrophe.

The causative force is our intellect and the soulless technology it has created, as well as our lawless and sense-disrupting technical culture, all of which are likewise the cause of the disruption of the Earth's water and blood circulation. If therefore everything created by this inorganic civilization perishes in the tempo of its own unfolding, then this decline is by no means a temporary crisis, but the inevitable collapse of a cultural edifice built to dizzying heights without any foundation, dragging down with it whatever genuine culture still remained.

Nature Protects Itself

The most effective protection of nature is the fallibility of man, his works, and his actions, the consequences of which must sooner or later destroy him, because a great part of his present-day actions run counter to all natural sense. Therefore it has always been only a question of the effectiveness of his actions and only ever a question of reaching a certain level of civilization, when the regressive movement begins and when everything built with much care and sweat must collapse in on itself again.

Once humanity has reached this level, nature will shed its greatest enemy entirely of its own accord, and with renewed power it will rebuild what humans have destroyed through their labor.

If therefore more and more people today are found who resist this terrible activity and commotion, this usually happens less out of love for nature than out of reasons of self-preservation, which has been preserved in humans as a force of nature.

The constant efforts of individuals who, with a far-reaching gaze into the future, are able to recognize the true face—the absurdity—of our work, because they have not yet lost their connection to nature, are indeed very serious but unfortunately little-heeded warnings to the rest of the world, which, swept along by the machinery of the times and

- 162 -
warped by overspecialization, can no longer perceive the details from which all life in nature is organically built and sustained, heartbeat by heartbeat. Unfortunately, these warnings to finally come to reason are merely voices crying in the wilderness.

Nature Knows Only Indirect Paths


---


How then should one do it differently? is the ever-repeated question. The answer is simple: Exactly the opposite of what is currently done. It requires only the simplest reflection to recognize that nature always takes indirect paths. Only we stubborn humans find it necessary to always take direct paths. We therefore should not complain when, in this manner, we fall into conflict with the almighty power of nature.

We would really have nothing else to do but to wisely fit ourselves into the marvelous order of nature, to understand that it is senseless and pointless to fight against these forces, and everything else would inevitably follow of its own accord. The improvement we all so fervently wish for would come quite on its own.

Many signs from nature constantly point us toward the right paths. However, these new paths lead in the opposite direction from the one we take today. This is only to be expected, since the current direction has led us to ruin.

The following exposition is intended to serve all those who have the earnest desire to take these paths.

The Essence of Water

The carrier of the cycle that sustains all life is water. In every drop of water dwells a deity whom we all ought to serve; dwells life, the soul of the first substance—water—whose banks and shores are the capillaries that guide us and in which it circulates.

Every watercourse, consisting of will and resistance, means constructive work and admonishes us to care for these vessels, the first and most important bodily forms, in which the product of a dual power—life—pulses.

Every heartbeat is a vein of this life, building its own channels and bridges before it, in order to guide the life developing in the earth in a way that accords with natural sense


---


- 163 -
to distribute and to carry to heights where it can first become light, beautiful, and free.

We humans, who stand at the highest level of this structure and, surpassing all else, are also blessed with intellect and reason, do the most foolish thing imaginable by constantly striving to regulate these watercourses from the banks—that is, to influence them mechanically rather than taking water into account as a living being.

How senseless this activity is becomes apparent from the mere consideration that the bank is a secondary result, while the primary thing is the being that forms it—namely, the water.

To regulate water from the banks is truly to fight the effects rather than the causes.

Just as it would never occur to a physician to mend breaking or cross-sectionally changing capillaries in the human body with thread and needle, so it should never occur to a thinking engineer to compact crumbling banks of a watercourse with ramming piles and brushwood, or to thoughtlessly smear cracks with cement.

Curiously, this is nevertheless done. Where these measures have practically led is shown by our entire river systems. In not a single case was the desired goal—the normal profile—achieved; on the contrary, all such river regulations brought about long-term damages that far outweigh the local and usually very short-lived advantages.

The great rivers—the Danube, the Rhine, the Tagliamento, the Adige, the Garonne, the Mississippi, and so on—bear witness to how incorrect the regulation works, carried out at enormous cost and with great diligence. Quite apart from the enormous damages triggered in the lower courses of these rivers by the purely mechanical regulation works, these watercourses must, through the usual treatment, lose precisely the most important thing: their great physical values.

The dirty gray sludge today called the Blue Danube, on whose bed river gold once gleamed; the Rhine, the symbol of German power, in which the Rhinegold once flashed—these are sad witnesses to these misguided measures. This gold of the Nibelungs was the rolling gravel at night, where the golden shimmer was caused by the pebbles rubbing against each other. For as the temperature of the water decreases, its tractive force increases, setting the bedload in motion. (If two pebbles are rubbed together under water, a golden glow appears. This yellow-red firelight was confused with gold supposedly lying at the bottom.)

This "gold" of the rivers now lies piled up in enormous gravel banks, which are perhaps occasionally shifted by the living force of the sluggish and dirty water masses flooding over them


---


- 164 -

be given, but no longer as in the past to give water "energies" and "soul," but only to help displace the soulless body "water" from its poorly regulated path.

Our clear, cold mountain streams have become wild waters, those merry companions who, as long as humans did not interfere, surrounded by blooming vegetation, savoring every blade of grass, can today no longer be tamed even with meter-thick cement walls.

Wherever we look, this terrible decay of life's bridges, of the capillaries and the bodies built by them appears, caused by the mechanical and senseless work of humans, which took the soul from the blood of the earth, the water.

And so it had to happen that the larger and more costly these regulation structures became, the greater the scale of the damages.

Nearly one million hectares of valuable agricultural land were lost in the lower reaches of the Danube through the regulation structures carried out in the upper reaches of this river. Similar conditions apply to all other rivers.

The more the engineer strives to lead the water, whose meaning and essence he still does not know today, into the sea by the straightest and shortest path, the more the watercourse lays itself into curves, the longer its path becomes, and the worse the water becomes.

The water flowing down the gradient is subject to a great inner law, whose power our water experts cannot begin to imagine.

Without this lawfulness, every watercourse would have to accelerate more and more and finally pass over into vapor form.

Science claims that water brakes itself through internal and external friction. But friction is known to be associated with heat generation.

Now, however, it shows that the temperature of faster-flowing water becomes lower, whereby it increases its carrying capacity and internal friction.

On the basis of this simple consideration, essential points of the theoretical complex of hydromechanics taught today become invalid.

Where then lies the actual secret of the steadiness of the flowing water masses?

The force that brakes the water flowing down the gradient is the energetic resistance that opposes Earth's gravity, the energy cycle working against the flow direction, which participates in those metabolic processes that give water its character and thus its soul. This important process is now prevented by the current type of watercourse regulation.

The logical consequence is the loss of its inner braking power. The water becomes soulless, thus characterless, and thereby also malicious.

- 165 -

The cancer-like decay of organisms


---


The more extensive these works become, which disturb the inner lawfulness of water, the greater the dangers for the banks and the surroundings; "water becoming characterless" smashes its own walls, because, having become unsteady, it seeks its soul with its last strength. The water masses come out of their proper path, the numerous energy bodies carried along by the water deposit themselves in the weary water, which now turns crosswise and robs the organic bodies of their soul. These organic bodies, deprived of their soul, their energy source, begin to rot, micro-life arises, and a cancerous decay sets in in the veins of the earth. The water seeping into the ground now poisons the groundwater. The water rising in the capillaries of the soil and the vegetation, the blood of the earth, carries the seeds of this terrible disease into the most diverse forms of vegetation. This results in a qualitative decay of this vegetation, first and foremost a decline of the trees of the forest, and subsequently the qualitative decline of everything in which the water circulates. Finally, slowly but surely, according to a law working with frightening persistence, we too come into the sequence. The spread of the most terrible of all diseases, cancer, is the necessary consequence of these unnatural regulation works. Naturally, other professional groups also contribute to this work of destruction.

The Forest

Among these professional groups, our modern forestry ranks first, which for a century has been vainly endeavoring to transform the forest, the highest plant organism, into a wood factory. The trees are placed in rows and ranks, regeneration and mixing ratios are arbitrarily changed. Nobody has any idea what goes on inside the tree and why the water, contrary to all mechanical laws, can rise with its substances in the capillaries of the trees. Some speak of osmotic pressure, others of an irritation of the root areas, all together are clear that ultimately the sun does it. Only the "how" is unclear to each individual. Once again, wish is the father of the thought. This too


---


- 166 -
research work is carried out mechanically and senselessly; for, firstly, every pumping installation needs a motor, secondly, it is not enough to tickle the trees at their toes, and thirdly, as is well known, trees cover themselves with branches, a sign that they wish to protect themselves from the sun and its direct thermal influence, because it can bestow its blessings only in an indirect way. But what does all this matter to our forester! He simply places the "shade-demanding" plants in the light, and behold, the trees grow more vigorously. Unfortunately, this magic too lasts only a short time. The structure of trees treated in this way becomes looser, more wide-meshed, and finally the same thing begins that is shown in our ever-dirtier channels. First, discolored spots appear in the cross-section, then the process of rot begins to spread from the inside outward, and inside the tree a life alien to the main organism arises in the most diverse forms and shapes — cancer — to which the macro-organism tree falls victim in the course of time. The various microbes are now diligently collected, given Latin names, and many people find rewarding employment in registering the countless diseases which surround the only health of the tree organism in ever greater numbers from year to year. Everyone overlooks that the sought-after originator of this new life is the senseless work of the forester.

Agriculture

Hand in hand with our foresters, the farmers work. The blood of the earth becomes ever more inferior, the soil declines in productivity, the necessity for fertilization has happily arrived. Now the chemist steps in and scatters his salts. Unfortunately, this good man too has no idea why and how these salts dissolve and in what way the energies arise that the plant needs for its becoming and thriving. Only a few years show a result, for after a short time the soils sprinkled with artificial fertilizer become slagged. Again the human has worked against nature and happily clogged the last source of nourishment, the capillaries of the soil. The farmer now stands helplessly before his field, which gave him abundant quantities in passing, while after a short time the quality of his fruits suffers, which he had formerly harvested in admittedly small but almost unlimited measure over time. Instinctively seeking the substances in the earth, he now deploys his deep plow and destroys the capillaries of the soil. And now the same thing occurs in

- 167 -
agriculture as in our forests. Outwardly everything seems to bloom and thrive. But these are only false blooms, which, nourished by the decaying marrow, now all the more bear the fruits of decay — cancer. The grain loses its content of starch substances, the meadows become mossy, the fields become weedy, only the labor and the costs increase. The end is the loss of the soil, the loss of the homeland.

The Energy Economy


---


The energy technician closes the round of this frenzy. Coal, the bread of the earth, and, where it still exists in sufficient measure, water, its blood, provide for the extraction of energies. Enormous quantities of energy, particularly electrical energy, are generated, but to this day no one knows what electricity actually is. The applications of electricity are very great, but unknown are the causes of its origin and the effects and consequences of today's methods of generating it. Only a few decades has the human been digging into this accidentally found wealth. Ever less and poorer becomes the driving water of his works, ever more colossal become the catastrophes on the earth, because the human has stolen from her the coal substances — her bread, her blood — the water, her soul — the energies. Unremittingly the human continues working, and ever greater grows his misery.

The Beginning Doubt

Ever more does exact science, the foundation of this method of working, begin to totter, ever greater grows the distrust of the people toward it. Ever deeper sinks the groundwater in the earth, ever worse become the climatic conditions, ever more hopeless our future, ever more characterless become the people. Only the need grows greater. Mothers begin to sell their love and their soul piece by piece on the street, fathers beg, brood over theft and murder, and ever more restless become the political conditions. Ever greater grows the stench of this senseless and purposeless economy, ever paler become the cheeks, and ever more garish the artificial red. Ever more terrible become the weapons of war, ever greater the fear of

- 168 -
the people of people. Every statistic reports the increase of the last and most dangerous disease, cancer. Helpless and at a loss, our doctors face this unchecked development. Countless are the victims of this terrible disease, the knife rages blindly, writhing in pain, the people rot in the hospitals. Nobody recognizes the causes of the origin of this terrible plague. Everything is registered, ordered, tortured innocent experimental animals, and the pathogen of our most dangerous enemy is sought. They will never and never find it this way, for it is our own work.

Questions for Science


---


And above all this, since the beginning, stands the sun and watches with icy silence the mad doing and dealing of the people who believe — and could it be otherwise given their immediate attitude — that it is a glowing ball. The closer we approach this warmth- and light-bestowing sun, the colder and darker becomes its face. The nearer we come to it, the clearer the stars become, and with the fading light of the sun the warmth, the atmosphere, the water, and life fade away. What then serves this sun as a carrier for light and warmth, if, according to the view of our scholars, space is empty of air? Why do light and warmth become more diffuse in the tropics, the light more intense at the poles, and their thermal radiation lesser? Why is the water at the bottom warmer at the poles? Why is it so icy cold at the sunlit surface? Why does the warm, light groundwater of the sea not rise high? Why are the water temperatures at the surface so warm at the equator? Why does it become colder toward the depths, and why does it become warmer again below the boundary layer of +4°C, and why does life begin there again? Why do the magnetic energy lines run from south to north, and why does the earth rotate from west to east? Why does the gyroscope stay upright when it is driven sideways? Why is the desert so dead despite all the heat? How can the warm Gulf Stream displace the cold seawater and travel thousands of kilometers against the temperature gradient without a mechanical gradient over hill and dale? Why does the groundwater rise so high above the ground surface in masonry? Why do wooden piles not rot under water, but always above water? Why do damp tiled roofs dry from the eaves to the ridge?


---


- 169 -
Why does rising cold water pierce the hardest stone?
Why does the warm air of the Earth not rise up?
Why is it so cold on the mountain peaks, which are closer to the Sun? Why
is it warmer at the ceiling in our homes and colder at the floor, when
heat is generated by an artificial heat source?
Why do gases contract when temperature decreases, and
why do the incandescent gases of the Sun not dissipate into space
at the presumed temperature of over 6000°C?
Why does marble expand when heated, and why does this
stone no longer contract under the influence of cold?
Why do West-East channels seed their banks? Why are the banks of an
East-West channel so bare? Why do South-North channels seed
themselves on one side only?
Why do channels flowing into cold seas drift laterally toward the North?
Why is the salt content of the seas different? Why do herring
migrate northward in winter?
Why do deep-sea fish glow?
Why do cold-blooded creatures carry the fever-producing poison?
Why does cold fever arise in the tropics?
Why does warm fever arise from chilling? What is fever,
after all?
What is temperature? What is heat? What is cold? What is energy?
Why does the heart beat in our chest? Who gives this muscle the
impulse for its movement? Where is the motor for this pump located?
Why does the blood circulate in our veins? Why do we breathe day and
night, in sleep and even in deep unconsciousness?
Why do the juices circulate in the hen's egg without a heart, why does the stone suffocate
when we cut off its air supply?
Why do light-wood species have thick bark and shade-wood species
only a thin rind?
Why does the trout stand motionless in the rushing stream, as if by magic?
How does the Earth remain suspended?
Does the heart beat because we breathe, or do we breathe because the heart beats?
Where is the heart in the plant?
Why does water pulse and breathe? Why does groundwater remain on
the mountain slopes, and why does it strive upward, becoming colder and heavier?
Why does it frequently spring from the highest
mountain peaks?
Why are there delta and lagoon formations?
What is evaporation, what is vaporization?
What is a solution, what is a compound, what is absorption, and what
effects underlie these processes?
Why do we develop subnormal temperature when we climb a mountain,
and supernormal temperature in the body when we descend?

- 170 -
The Error of Civilization


---


Is there really such a great difference between the crumbling banks and the bursting
of our blood vessels? Must the last man truly rot alive before we come to our senses
and to the realization that we have all been mistaken together?
Why do we not want to recognize that it is our senseless work that
is destroying us? Are we really entitled to drill such fundamentally baseless knowledge into our children,
when this very science has already led us to the very edge of the abyss? Where
does our knowledge begin, where does it end?
Does anyone really still dare to speak of science and culture? Are our children really wrong
when they no longer want to be advised by their parents and teachers and go their own ways? Does anyone seriously believe
that political phrases and bayonets can still hunger? Are there really still
people who hope for improvement through forced labor, when even voluntarily performed labor has already brought such unspeakable misfortune?
If that is so, then may Nature only continue to wield its power in peace, for then
it is doing a great and noble work.
Nature is simpler in its effects and more complicated in its impulses than we can fathom with our categorical understanding.

An Experiment

A small and unremarkable experiment in itself shows us a great law.

Take a vessel, fill it with sand, insulate this vessel at the sides
and bottom against external temperature influences.
By placing ice at the bottom of the vessel, a temperature of
+4°C is artificially produced, i.e., conditions are created such as we find them in the Earth.
Into this prepared vessel, insert a U-shaped bent glass tube, into which, along with some pure quartz sand (which is chemically nearly inert), good water, rich in absorbed and dissolved carbons, that has not been exposed to the light of the Sun, is filled.
On the open ends of the U-tube, two glass caps are placed, one with two capillaries, the other with four capillaries, in an open


---


- 171 -
connection has been fused.
Once all this has been done, allow the Sun's rays to act upon the surface of the sand bed. When the water filled in the U-tube, through the ice-cooled sand layer in the lower position, reaches the anomaly point of +4°C, and the surface of the vessel, under the influence of the Sun shining upon it, reaches approximately +20°C, the water — which as is known reaches its greatest density and weight at +4°C — begins to lose its equilibrium and rises in one of these tubes, if the two U-tube legs are transitioned into appropriately tapered forms.
Now, if air is allowed to enter this U-tube through openings arranged on both sides — as happens, for example, through the boreholes or wells that we drive into the Earth — the water column, which stands higher on one side, sinks, and the water equalizes in both U-tube legs according to the law of communicating vessels.
Why does the water sink when it is brought into contact with the entering atmosphere?
If one closes the two openings again from atmospheric influence and the cold ambient temperatures act once more, the water begins to rise again slowly after some time.
At night, the process reverses, i.e., in the capillaries in which the water rises under the influence of light and heat, calm sets in, while the water in the remaining capillaries now rises. The rising product of the mutual equalization processes corresponds exactly to the difference that prevails between day and night.
This simple experiment shows us why different substances rise in plants during day and night, why different blood circulates in our veins. But it also shows us something of the secret of life and its origin, which is created only through opposites, only through heat and cold.
At the same time, this experiment shows us the absurdity of our purely mechanical and thoroughly one-sided actions, which we perform as work, without knowing the inner laws and their processes.
It would go too far to explain the necessary details and the prerequisites for the success of this experiment.
Once again, it must be emphasized that every development of life and the associated body-building process is not, as humanity today assumes, only a heat process, but also a cold process; for only opposites can give birth to life.
At this point, it is also not possible to show the subtle differences that exist in every individual decomposition and transformation process, e.g., during growth, and to explain the prerequisites that are necessary, for example, to transform such energy bodies as coal, metals, minerals, elements and their compounds into


---


- 172 -
can.
It would also go far too far to explain exhaustively how one is able to collect decomposed energy components and, so to speak, re-embody them immaterially.
One thing, however, can be said: Our scholars may as well give up smashing atoms by violent means in order to obtain free energy forms from material energy. These attempts are pointless and contrary to purpose.
Nature shows us, in every blade of grass, how to do it more simply and more wisely.

The Path to Free Energies

In every drop of good spring water, there are more forces present than a medium-sized power plant of the present day is capable of producing.
These energies can be obtained with little effort and almost free of charge, if we follow the paths that Nature constantly shows us and abandon the wrong paths that our modern technology pursues.
Happiness and health, as well as unlimited quantities of energy, are available to us almost free of charge, once we recognize that in water, in the blood of the Earth, the will and its resistance, life, dwell — the life for which we today struggle so hard, because we constantly rob this carrier of all life, through our actions, of its most precious quality: its soul.
The will of Nature is the construction that serves the whole, proceeding through the path of atomic decomposition and atomic transformation. Its resistance is our stubborn, atom-smashing work, the egoistic exploitation of Nature.
The only possible consequence of our purely categorical division of the intellect, which is forced upon us as children in school, is the loss of creative activity. The human being loses his individuality, the ability to look at the thing in itself, and with it the interconnectedness of Nature. He approaches the state of equilibrium that is impossible in Nature, which must inevitably lead to an overall economic decline. Therefore, the laws on which we base our actions are also incorrect, because they operate within boundaries that do not exist.
The work we perform is our embodied will. The soul of this work is its effect. It brings happiness when this work is done correctly, and reliably brings misery when this work has been done incorrectly.
Human! Only your will can ever come to pass, for you are the master of Nature when you follow her. Do not complain when you become her servant.
