The Pulsation of Water

Life plays out in three spheres.

1. In the carbon sphere,
2. in the atmosphere,
3. in the stratosphere.


---


The connection between these spheres is established by water. On the
other hand, the different states of aggregation of water form the bridges
for the building-up and transformation of the elemental substances
carried by the water, which pass from the stratosphere into the interior of
the earth and, conversely, from the interior of the earth into the
stratosphere.
Opposed to the purely mechanical circulation of the body-form "water" is
the so-called energy circulation. The rising of the carbon substances with
the carrier water stands opposite the sinking of the oxygen. At the
intersection of the paths of these currents directed oppositely toward
each other, energy is released.
The energy equalization processes cannot lead to a state of equilibrium
owing to the constant change in the duration of day and night, so that
there must constantly be shifts in the individual microclimatic
conditions, and thereby, in turn, constantly shifts in the quantities and
qualities of the elemental substances.
The consequence of the constant interactions is, on the one hand, the
transformation of the types of water located in the individual zones, and
on the other hand, the constant transformation of the forms of
vegetation through which the water, moved uninterruptedly by this inner
interplay of forces, draws its way.
Opposed to the inner equalization of forces is now the effect of the
weight of the water. Through the change in the magnitude of the force
components, there must come about a constant rising and falling of the
water particles, the so-called pulsation of the water.
Every new formation and every building-up derives from the smallest
beginnings. A further development in the first stages is able to take
place only by way of a correctly unfolding circulation in the interior of
the earth. Every higher form of vegetation builds itself up, according to
law, upon the lower vegetation that preceded it. The carrier of the
substances and the mediator of the life processes in the so-called root
zone is the groundwater. It receives the impulse for its movement from
the drop in temperature, which in turn is caused by the inner metabolic
processes of the groups of elemental substances that are decisive for
this. The impulse for the movement of the water is therefore a product
of the equalization processes between the opposites contained in the
water, which find the corresponding resistance in the water itself.
Through the resistance that the water offers to the equalization
processes


---


- 205 -
between the carbon substances and the oxygen, constant temperature
fluctuations arise once more, and with them again the impulse for the
movement, the pulsation of the water, which on its way now dissolves
salts, now deposits salts, transports them, forms and transforms energies.
The meaning and purpose of these eternal processes of transformation is
the building-up and preservation of the various forms of vegetation and of
bodily form, which in turn represent the bridges for the building-up and
preservation of the energies.
The differences in tension that constantly exist between the inner and
the ambient temperature are nothing other than forms of force that close
the water's circulation and at the same time kindle it anew.
The forms of development are thus 1. of material (bodily), 2. of
immaterial (soul-like) nature.
Everything that exists, be it a stone, a plant, an animal, a human being,
some planet or the sun, represents an organism that thus has both body
and soul.
Every ray of light and warmth requires a bodily form in which it can
form and organize itself.
Every body needs an inner energy that builds it up and transforms it. If
a body decays, then those forces that built it up are set free once again.
They are never lost; for if they lose their home with the decaying body,
the water that eternally circulates in, on and above the earth takes them
up willingly and carries them onward to a new life.
Wherever we look, there is life as eternal building-up and rebuilding. If
we look into apparent emptiness, a sea of soul-life, past and coming
generations, gazes back at us.
To every material form of vegetation there always stands opposed an
immaterial form, the light, the warmth, the radiation. Every change of the
sphere alters the outer and inner overall conditions, changes the weight
and the inner radiation intensity of the bodily substance "water" and
with it the direction of movement of the carrier of life.
Disturbances of the inner and outer laws lead to the disturbance of the
formation of the entire structure of life.
The vanishing of the water, or its substantive transformation, is a very
serious warning sign, because with the change of the inner composition the
character of the water also changes, and with it the character of all life
forms, including that of the human being.
The qualitative decline of our forms of vegetation, above all the
qualitative decay of the highest plant organisation, the forest, the
physical and moral decay of humanity, are only a consistent consequence
of the disturbance of the physical composition of the water and of the
disturbance of the geosphere, brought about by the burrowing work of
the human being in the organism Earth.
The religion of the Chinese forbids any intrusion into the earth.


---


- 206 -
Even the building of railways encountered great obstacles of a religious
nature in China. In light of what has been set out so far, it can be
recognised that the culture of the Chinese, which has outlived all other
peoples, is no accident, but has its cause in the fact that the coal sphere
remained untouched for a long time. The decline of China had to begin at
the very moment when they too adopted the customs, usages and technical
achievements of the Occident.
A terrible example is also presented by the development in Russia, which
in 15 years caught up with what the other states achieved in almost 100
years — the famine.
What we are therefore experiencing now is not a crisis, but the dying of
the whole, the qualitative bodily decay of all organisms that sets in
through the disturbance of the water balance in nature.
In step with it goes the moral, psychic and mental collapse of humanity,
which has already gone so far that, despite all warning signs, people still
fail to recognise the seriousness of the situation and, worse than
animals, see their last salvation in the decimation of the masses of
mankind with the weapon, which our priests, together with the flags under
which our children are to bleed to death, even still bless.
The decision whether we go this last path or can preserve ourselves in
the final hour from our own self-destruction lies only with ourselves, or
rather with the men of science and of the state, who take upon themselves
a truly dreadful responsibility if, out of self-interested motives,
without regard for the seriousness of the situation and without being able
to bring real help, they persist in their standpoint.

The healing water for humans, animals and soil

As long as the human being had not disturbed the organic interrelations,
and Mother Earth could still bestow her blood, the water, upon vegetative
life in a healthy state, there was no occasion to think about how healthy
water can be prepared by artificial means — but in the way that the
earth produces it.
Today, however, when almost all healthy springs have either run dry or
the water is already intercepted at its source and conveyed to the
settlements in wrongly built pipelines, when the soil and the entire
animal world are dependent on used-up, stale and therefore unhealthy
water, indeed even for human use water laden with still lower-organised
constituents, immature water, must be torn from the womb of the earth, or
surface water rendered harmful to health by chemical additives and
sterilisation must be supplied to the dwellings, it is high time to find
means and ways,


---


- 207 -
to protect humans, animals and soil from the decay that must lawfully
occur if the earth, through the inner decomposition process of the water
brought about by today's economic measures, dies of thirst.
Only nature can and may be our great teacher. If we wish to recover in
mind and body, we must not rely merely on mechanical or hydraulic
side-effects, but must above all take care to fathom the great lawful
processes — how and in what manner Mother Earth prepares her blood,
the water, and conveys it to the places of consumption.
Once we have unveiled this secret and faithfully imitate what has been
proven over millions of years, then we are infallible, and only then can
we meaningfully intervene in the great life-work of nature and reap in
abundance the finest and noblest fruits that Mother Earth builds up and
preserves in countless variations with the help of a healthy blood.
In order to fathom the great mystery of the origin of all life, we must
strive not only to study the space in which we live, but must also take
an interest in what is "above and below", in which the water, obeying a
great law, takes its eternal cycle.
Even if it is impossible for us to behold the wonderful processes in the
crystal-clear water with the eye, and impossible to accompany the water
on its mysterious paths above and below the earth, there still remains
open the indirect, the inductive path, to research that which we cannot
see but absolutely must know if we wish to remain healthy and thereby
serve the purpose of life, the constant building-up.
The human being has so far committed only crimes against Mother Earth
and thereby inflicted severe damage not only on himself but also on his
environment. With infinite patience she has for a time tolerated his
interventions guided by greed, avarice and lack of understanding. Now,
however, since as a result of the constant churning and aeration of the
earth an inner decomposition of her blood and thus a dying of the
substances that nourish us comes about — because people not only attach
their pumping works to the inner circulation of the water and tear the
water from the womb of the earth, but also ruin the water flowing on the
earth's surface through senseless regulation of the watercourses, and
take the forest from Mother Earth or destroy it organically — the day
of reckoning has finally come for the human being himself.
This moment had to come, in order to bring humanity to the insight that
nothing on earth goes unpunished, and that every senseless intervention
in the wonderful life-work "nature" must in the end take its revenge
upon the human being himself.
The fairy tale of the former paradise is no empty delusion. Even if


---


- 208 -
our ancestors, though constantly struggling with the difficulties of
life, nevertheless still had carefree days compared to the present time.
But what will it look like after another generation, if things continue
downhill at the pace they have gone so far? What future are our children
heading toward, if no means is found to halt this terrible decay?
We already stand before phenomena that must shake every seriously
thinking person to their innermost being. What purpose does it serve to
keep lying to ourselves, or to give ourselves over senselessly to the
hope that somehow it will get better again on its own?
If we wish to make life beautiful and desirable once more, then we must
set the lever where life begins. The origin of life, the primordial
substance, is the water, which harbours the secret of all becoming. We
will only be able to unveil this secret when we learn to understand the
innermost essence of water.
Just as the ripe apple falls from the tree, so the water rises of its own
accord out of the earth when it is ripe — that is, when the water has so
transformed itself inwardly that it can and must leave Mother Earth
again, overcoming its own body weight.
Although the correct capping of springs cannot be gone into further
here, reference should at least be made to the art of the ancients, which,
like much else, was lost or had to give way to something worse. The
capping of springs was carried out by the Romans as far as possible in
such a way that, at a certain height above the spring's mouth, they
placed a covering in the form of a thick-walled stone slab on the
carefully levelled natural rock at the side; into this stone slab, which
was completely sealed at the sides by wedges, a hole was hewn, into which
the drainage pipes were inserted in such a way that no access of air was
possible. All types of these spring cappings of that time, despite and
precisely because of their simplicity, took more account of the nature of
the water than today's spring-capping systems, which, apart from other
serious errors, often destroy the water circulation and metabolic
conditions between the spring and its surroundings through excessive
structural measures and disturbances in the vicinity of the spring, and
through the use of lime, cement or metal casings.
Regarding the choice of material for the conduits, where wood was not
used, the coin metal thrown into the spring for cultic reasons was
observed, and that which best withstood years of influence was chosen for
the conveying of the water. Depending on the nature of the water, some of
the sacrificed metals were quite literally incrusted by the water — that
is to say, as it were not accepted by the deity — while others were
again almost entirely dissolved, a sign that water also effortlessly
decomposes the metals that every body needs for its own building-up, and
that every cult has its deeper meaning.


---


- 209 -

What is said in the following should and can be no recipe for how to
produce healthy water. It is intended only to say here that the thinking
human being, in this field too, can make good the sins of his fathers, and
is in a position to produce good, healthy water for himself, just as the
Earth brings it forth.

Just as it is self-evident to us that from the healthy seed placed in the
ground a mighty tree can ultimately develop, so it must also become
self-evident that only mature and healthy water brings forth healthy fruit.

Just as the seed grain laid in the moist earth requires for its further
development warmth and cold, light and shadow, and the energies
connected with them, so it is exactly the same with water, which likewise
needs these opposites in order to rebuild and reconstitute itself inwardly.
To obtain these necessary opposites it makes its long journey through
the universe.

In every drop of good water dwells a world of possibilities. That, too,
which we imagine as God has its home in every drop of water. If we
destroy the water, if we take from it its cradle, the forest, then we
senselessly rob ourselves of the highest goods of life, health, and thereby
lose also the place of our work, our homeland. Restless, like the water
whose soul has been taken from it, we too must then reach for the
wanderer's staff. Wherever we arrive, decay begins, unrest begins, ruin
begins, need and misery begin.

But if our work is not to become a curse but to lead to a blessing, then
we must content ourselves with living from the interest and the matured,
ripened capital products, never from the substance of the Earth directly.
This interest the water offers in so valuable a form that we can calmly
renounce everything else if we understand the household of the Earth
and nourish ourselves only from its abundance, and take only what is
ripe. It is not yet too late; we still have water. Let us finally tend this
giver of life, and everything will become good again of its own accord!

Good high-spring water differs from atmospheric water (rain water) in its
inner content of matter. High-spring water possesses, besides the
dissolved salts, a relatively high proportion of gases in free and bound
form (carbonic acid). The gases absorbed by a good high-spring water
consist to 96 percent of carbon-stuff groups. By carbon-stuffs are meant
here all the carbons of the chemist, all elements and their compounds, all
metals and minerals — in a word, all substances with the exception of
oxygen and hydrogen.

Atmospheric water (rain water, aqua destillata, condensation water, or
water exposed in channels to strong aeration and intensive light
influence), that is to say surface water, shows a


---


- 210 -

comparatively high content of oxygen, nearly no, or only lowly organized
salt forms, no or only little free and little bound carbonic acid, and a gas
content absorbed from the air that consists predominantly of oxygen
dissolved in physical form.

By physical form of solution is to be understood a higher form of solution
(combination) of various groups of substances than occurs with purely
chemical forms of solution. With the physical form of solution, energetic
processes are already actively at work.

We accordingly distinguish water that possesses a high percentage of
carbon-stuff energies, and water that exhibits a high percentage of
oxygen energies. The former we shall call cathode water, the second
anode water. Cathode water possesses negative, anode water positive
forms of energy.

These forms of energy are characteristic of what we designate as the
sphere, psyche, or character of the water.

The high-spring water bubbling up out of the Earth accordingly possesses
to a predominant degree a carbon sphere, negative forms of energy or
negative character; the rain water coming from the atmosphere possesses
predominantly an oxygen sphere, positive forms of energy or positive
character.

The water sinking from the atmosphere into the Earth requires, for its
inner transformation, besides the possibility of taking up certain
transformative substances and the necessary exclusion of light and air,
also certain path lengths and spans of time in order to be able to carry
out the transformation process properly — that is, in order to become
inwardly ripe.

The water is ripe when the air absorbed by it contains at least 96 percent
carbon sphere, and the proportion of solid carbon-stuffs belonging to that
sphere.

The quality and the inner rising force of the water depend on this inner
ripeness.

The longer the path covered, the more highly organized and qualitatively
more valuable its inner energy, its character becomes — provided the
presence of the appropriate transformative substances.

The nearer to the center of the Earth, the more highly organized and more
aggressive the oxygen groups sinking with the water become.

When atmospheric water sinks into the Earth, as it approaches the
geothermal depth level of + 4 degrees Celsius a concentration of its
oxygen content takes place, while all carbon-stuffs present above this
boundary layer, which equalize themselves with the oxygen particles as
they approach this layer, are transformed and rise up in part as nitrogens,
in part remain behind as salt crystals.

The water charged with oxygen can therefore carry no already elevated
carbon-stuffs below the boundary layer of + 4 degrees Celsius, but must,
through the inverted process


---


- 211 -

leave behind, in the vegetation layer, the carbon-stuffs that were earlier
brought up from the interior of the Earth. This vegetation layer represents,
so to speak, the small depot, which through these transformation processes
is constantly supplied from above and below with oxygen and with
carbon-stuffs respectively, and which is bounded below by the geothermal
zero layer of + 4 degrees Celsius.

The water sinking further beyond the boundary layer can go deep only with
those surplus or lowly organized oxygen portions that, for lack of the
presence of correspondingly organized carbon-stuffs in the vegetation
zone, could enter into no equalizing and no transformation processes
(oxidations) whatsoever.

Through the temperatures in the interior of the Earth, which increase with
growing depth and which in turn have themselves arisen through these
equalizing processes, the oxygens sinking with the water become ever
more aggressive, whereby the equalization and transformation of these
oxygen groups with the more highly organized carbon-stuffs found at
increasing depth is made possible, and finally even the carbon exhibiting a
solid state of aggregation is decomposed and transformed, when the
aggressive oxygen, under the simultaneously arising high pressure, comes
into direct contact with the carbon. Something similar, incidentally, we
find also in the conversion of foodstuffs in our body, which, as is well
known, proceeds in various ways with the intake of water and air, and
carries out the transformation processes that condition life.

The higher the transformed and upgraded carbon-stuffs rise toward the
surface of the Earth, the lower the surrounding temperatures become as
the boundary layer of + 4 degrees Celsius is approached. In connection
with this, the oxygen portions of the ground water also become less
aggressive again.

The more valuable the carbon-stuffs are, the more deeply organized the
oxygen groups can be in order to carry out the equalization process, and
conversely.

Through the Earth temperatures that change with sunrise, sunset, and
the change of the seasons, the boundary layer of + 4 degrees Celsius also
changes its respective position — that is, by day it lies in general deeper,
by night higher.

In assessing the causes of the fluctuations of the ground-water table, the
introduction of the already familiar concept of "saturation deficit" is
necessary, by which the relationship between the temperature of the
atmosphere and its content of water vapor is indicated.

The climatic conditions of Central Europe are of a moderately continental
character and are distinguished by maximum precipitation quantities in
the summer months. But standing against this, corresponding to the
higher temperature, there is also an increased evaporation — that is,


---


- 212 -

the saturation deficit becomes greater.

From the climatology of Hann we gather that the yearly distribution of
rain in Central Europe amounts to 9 to 13 percent in the summer months,
and 4 to 6 percent in the winter months. Standing against these values of
precipitation distribution, according to the investigations of Mayer
(Meteorologische Zeitung 1887), are saturation deficits in summer of 3 to
7 mm, and in winter those of 0.3 to 1.0 mm.

From the tables contained in the textbook of Keilhack it is evident that the
water content of the air, at the same measure of relative humidity, can
rise to more than 15-fold with temperature increases from -10 degrees to
+ 30 degrees. Only through the indication of precipitation quantities and
saturation deficit can one arrive at laws for the fluctuations of the
ground-water table. Since now these two meteorological components in
general neither sum directly nor cancel each other out, the fluctuations of
the ground-water table must therefore depend above all on the mutual
relationship of the two.

The practical possibilities of application of the laws prevailing here
emerge in the labor-free and nearly cost-free raising of the ground-water
table sunk deep in the deserts — which problem will be discussed still
more precisely and clarified completely in a later treatise.

Standing against the mechanical interplay of forces that is active in the
raising and lowering of the ground-water table is the physical
equalization, the absorption of the carbon-stuff portions, the binding of
the gaseous carbon-stuffs, which at the appropriate temperature, under
exclusion of light and outside air, disperse themselves in the water
(disperse).

Standing against the oxygen concentration occurring under these
circumstances is locally always the highest dispersion of the carbon-stuff
groups — that is, the water can complete its transformation and become
inwardly ripe.

The water standing above the boundary layer now charges itself further
with the carbon-stuffs located in the vegetation space of the Earth,
consumes finally ever more of its oxygen, and must, when it has reached a
certain degree of saturation, in consequence of the Earth temperatures
that become warmer again in summer as the surface is approached, let
carbonic acid escape, which rises up in the form of pearls and now also
helps lift the water mechanically in the capillaries.

Standing against this interplay of forces again is a physical form of
energy, the oxygen hunger of the water supersaturated with the noblest
carbon-stuffs, whereby a negative pressure, and thereby the rising of the
water, is co-caused.

The good high-springs bubble up not, as has hitherto been assumed,
through mechanical overpressure, but through the negative-pressure
phenomena that ultimately arise from the material transformation
processes.


---


- 213 -

In this way the phenomenon of the high mountain springs that rise on the
peaks, or at least at great altitude, also explains itself; under the
action of physical opposites they are induced to climb upward.

If the carbon substances, which improve steadily as they rise, finally
approach the oxygen concentration present in the high regions of the
air, the last accompanying water crystallizes out as fine ice under the
low temperatures prevailing there and sinks down together with the
oxygen. The carbon substance particles, which continue to rise ever
higher, now moving without any carrier, extremely dispersed, finally
reach the highest oxygen concentration, the sun, and now contribute to
the organic building-up process of the solar system. The reverse case
takes place in the depths of the earth, where the already compact,
concentrated carbon substance groups standing under excess pressure —
the coal — are decomposed under the influence of the most aggressive
oxygen.

The energies arising in the high regions between the most highly
organized carbon substance groups and the lower-organized oxygen
quantities return to the earth by way of radiation, and conversely the
radiation energies released in the depths of the earth draw upward.

Through the resistance that the gaseous hydrogen, becoming denser with
approach to the earth, opposes to these equalization processes, these
energies are transformed into light, or respectively heat radiations, in
which form they finally reach the earth again and contribute to the
organic build-up of the forms of vegetation. The processes taking place
in the depths of the earth are of an oppositely directed nature.

Radiation, light and heat are thus counterparts to certain energy forms
appearing on the surface of the earth.

The vegetation, the material bodies, are likewise the result of
continuously ongoing transformation processes, everywhere the water
draws, with whose help the necessary equalization processes take place.
Every change in the forms of vegetation must therefore inevitably lead
to a change in the inner transformation and build-up, to a change in the
climatic conditions and thereby to a change in the inner character of
the blood of the world, the water. The properties, or respectively the
character of the blood of the world, are conditioned by a sum of
circumstances of which only the smaller part has been heeded by our
specialists.

The advantageous or disadvantageous influences of certain substances
contained in the water, such as chlorine, ammonia, manganese, iron,
sulphuric acid, etc., shall not be entered into here, since the
specialist literature is broad enough on this point.

From our standpoint, what interests us above all is the content of
carbonic acid in its variously bound forms together with their salts


---


- 214 -

and the content of oxygen.

Slowly but surely one can now already see from various publications
that attention is also being paid to those compounds contained in the
water that occur in a certain labile state form. Great temperature
changes, the influence of air and light can destroy these sensitive
formations, on which however it essentially depends, within a short
time. The latter remarks apply to ordinary drinking water above all to
the half-bound carbonic acid, as contained in the bicarbonate salts.
But the so-called free carbonic acid is also of great significance,
since on the one hand it is an essential contributing cause of the
refreshing taste of good spring water, and on the other hand, as the
so-called "associated" free carbonic acid, it must contribute to
keeping the labile bicarbonate salts in solution. A content of free
carbonic acid going beyond this gives the water aggressive properties,
and this has a particularly disadvantageous effect on metal walls in
the simultaneous presence of oxygen.

The significance that attaches to the supply of air, or respectively to
the exclusion of air, is also evident from the fact that, for example,
pyrite in groundwater does not decompose under exclusion of air. The
moment oxygen can enter through the influence of human hands, sulphuric
acid forms from the iron pyrites. As is well known, to this day it has
not been possible to ship certain medicinal waters while retaining
their effect. In those waters that contain certain labile iron
compounds, to which indeed a part of the effectiveness is due, the
decomposition phenomena that occur with access to air and light have
already been demonstrated, although at first it seemed as if everything
in the water had been preserved qualitatively and quantitatively.

All radioactive waters also suffer a high loss of their healing effect
a longer time after their emergence from the spring mouth; their
emanation activity is strongest at the beginning and can be maintained
when carried in pipelines only with the application of very special
precautionary measures. The same naturally applies to other waters as
well.

According to the opinion of Professor Dittler, the radioactive gas is
mechanically admixed to the medicinal water and loses half of its
activity within four days already.

The oxygen content of the water amounts, depending on the temperature,
according to L. Winkler, to 6 to 8 cubic centimeters per 1 liter of
water. Compared to the quantity of carbonic acid soluble in 1 liter of
water, which amounts to between 1500 and 1000 cubic centimeters
depending on the temperature from 4 degrees to 15 degrees, this
quantity is very small.

In general, care should be taken that the hydrogen ion concentration
does not fall below 0.2*10 = 7, since the


---


- 215 -

aggressiveness of the oxygen leads to damage to pipelines.

Carbonates dissolved in the water are then also precipitated under the
influence of the action of the oxygen.

Experiments that were carried out to clarify the connection between the
temperature of the water and purely mechanical external circumstances
delivered no satisfactory result. Kerner attempted to set up formulas
that give the spring warmth as a function of the petrographic character
of the mountains and the altitude above sea level. Thus, for example,
for springs at the foot of dolomitic surface moraines he gives the
formula:

t = 8.00 - 0.31 h, so that with an increase in altitude of about 200 to
300 m the temperature of the water would decrease by 1 degree.

But J. Stiny also points out that the functional connection between
altitude and water temperature is not to be taken too strictly, since
many other circumstances, among them the "exposure to air paths," etc.,
cooperate.

Keilhack points to the heat influences that are active in oxidation and
hydrate formation processes in the water itself, whereby these
quantities of heat carry a considerable significance. Where carbon
substances occur in concentrated form as hard coal or lignite, the heat
effect conditioned by the oxidation or respectively combustion
phenomena of the coal seams in the interior of the earth is added.

A phenomenon occurring here and there is that springs yield cooler
water in summer and rise higher than in winter. In summer there is a
positive temperature gradient from the atmosphere to the lithosphere.
At this time the cold snow-melt water, which was strongly
oxygen-containing, comes to light. In winter there is a negative
temperature gradient from the atmosphere to the lithosphere, and the
frozen ground prevents the penetration of surface water, so that the
water which seeped in during the summer, comparatively less rich in
oxygen, comes from the depths. In both cases the water had a long time
to charge itself with carbon substances and to transform and refine
itself correspondingly under the influence of a suitable temperature
gradient, so that such springs deliver excellent water. Besides the
span of time that the water has available for the refining processes in
the interior of the earth, the oxygen content of the starting water is
therefore also decisive, because with water richer in oxygen the
transformation processes take place more vigorously, if the water can
reach deep layers. Since the snow-melt water sinking deep into the cool
soil layers shows a greater oxygen content than ordinary precipitation
water, the cooler water emerging in summer must therefore also be
qualitatively more valuable.

Many of the hydraulic insights of today, considered purely mechanically
and almost not physically, will, when consideration is given to the


---


- 216 -

physical moments indicated so far, lead to quite different conclusions.
This principled standpoint of the physical and not merely mechanical
way of viewing also leads to the fact that my insights can never be
fitted into today's hydraulic complex of ideas, and that people will not
understand me for as long as they hold fast to the previous, merely
one-sided ways of viewing.

What has been said above naturally also applies to the present-day
regulations of watercourses and in particular also to the inner
destruction of the character of water when water is used as a working
substance for machines, etc.

What devastating consequences the modern forestry industry, for
example, has caused through its clear-cutting and light-felling and the
soil temperatures thereby altered, shall be discussed in a separate
chapter.

The transformation processes constantly taking place in nature can be
imitated artificially without further ado for the attainment of a
healthy mature water, if the suitable body forms are created in which
the necessary transformation processes can take place.

Chemistry too has in recent years come to the recognition that it is
absolutely not sufficient to characterize a water, or respectively a
medicinal water, by its salt constituents given qualitatively and
quantitatively.

The change in the freezing and boiling point that certain waters showed
led to the recognition that the freezing point of aqueous solutions
depends on the number of molecules contained in 1 liter of water. Only
electrochemistry came onto an approximately correct path, in that it
began to show what really matters here.

While the solution of many organic substances (organic in the sense of
today's chemistry) conducts the electric current only slightly or not at
all, precisely the substances contained in waters (carbon substances)
count among the electrolytes.

In the correctly carried out solution of the carbon substance groups
characteristic of waters and medicinal waters, ionization also occurs
without the passage of a weak current. Since it has been possible to
conduct electric current through aqueous solutions, whereby the
ionization of the salt solutions naturally occurs without any
demonstrable loss of electrical energy taking place, a proof is thereby
given for the above axiom. This phenomenon becomes still more
understandable and also gains practical value when one is familiar with
the explanation given by me elsewhere about the actual nature of
electricity.

The mode of representing waters in which the salts are given in their
dissociated form is therefore to be described as a very small advance,
and the remaining energetic processes that take place in the water, of
which I have so far only hinted


---


- 217 -
spoken of, is by no means exhausted.
The relevant clarifications of these processes will change today's
views and the practical application possibilities of so-called
electricity from the ground up and confront humanity with
undreamed-of possibilities for development.
Even if it is not yet possible at present, for purely patent-law
reasons, to describe the apparatuses for producing healthy water in
more detail, the path by which it is possible, besides the preparation
of healthy water, to obtain all other forms of energy, whatever they
may be called, directly from water by mechanical-physical means,
should already be visible.
Had our scientists, instead of pursuing ever more stubborn goals,
taken nature as their teacher, today's misery would undoubtedly have
been spared us. Objectively judging experts and justly thinking
scientists will realize that it is high time to remedy, in the
interest of the embattled masses, the many errors and mistakes
committed so far — some of them only recently, some inherited from
of old — as quickly as possible, and they will also concede that it
will not do to lose unnecessary time with this necessary conversion
and to wait until, perhaps, the sluggish scientific apparatus has
laboriously adapted itself to the new guidelines.
Decades of study and thorough observation have yielded not only the
necessary overview but also all the practical aids required for it,
so that the public can today already be confronted with practical
facts, and what has been said above can be proven to it by means of
well-constructed apparatuses.

Water in Cult, in Life, and in Medicine

The following section shall be devoted to the significance that
water held in the life and cult of our ancestors, whereby the
symbol-historical investigation is partly borrowed from the
expositions of Martin Ninck, Weinhold, Norden, and others.
Modern man, who indeed has little or no time left for inner
contemplation and developmental-historical considerations, naturally
sees in water only a purely chemical substance, which is just about
good enough for his bodily needs as bathing and utility water, for
driving power stations. A wholly different attitude toward water was
shown by our ancestors, who saw in it the source of life.
Many legends and traditions from the mythology of the various
peoples harbor a far deeper meaning than the ever more sober


---


- 218 -
posterity, ever more sober but less deeply thinking, is willing to
grant it.
The standpoint represented in my expositions — to regard water as
the blood of the Earth — finds its parallel in certain points of our
ancestral cult. Various legends and depictions point to the mother
blood, the mother's milk, the mother's tears of our all-mother Earth.
Even the linguistics of modern times still betrays something of the
symbolism of earlier times. Thus, for example, it is no accident that
the word "Quelle" (spring/source) takes a feminine article (die
Quelle).
The figure of the water goddesses, the nymphs, is always linked with
love stories. The nymphs are at all times ready to give birth, just as
Goethe also says of the spring in "Faust": "There a spring of
intoxicated songs unceasingly gave birth to itself anew."
Wuttke-Meyer also connects the following old German custom with the
fertility of the spring. Every pregnant woman who goes to the spring
for the first time must "silver" it by throwing a coin into it,
otherwise the spring would run dry.
Besides the springs, however, the rivers and lakes also enjoy high
esteem in the cult of the ancients. Even today we find in allegorical
depictions protective deities of a country's principal river as its
emblem. Depending on whether the water is at rest or in motion,
female or male fertility potency is ascribed to it. In Chant de la
Saussé's history of religion we find that, according to Egyptian
belief, the primeval water "NUN" carries a double potency within
itself.
In the sayings "God, Soul, and World" Goethe says:
"Where the water divides, living things are first set free. And when
the water unfolds, it immediately takes on living form. There animals
roll, they dry into flora, and plant branches they push forth."
And in "Faust" the same German prince of poets says: "You springs of
all life, on which Heaven and Earth depend, you well up, you
moisten!"
To illustrate the significance of water in the healing arts, it is
probably best to let a physician still bound to nature speak for
himself.
Dr. Schew writes: Water is nature's great giver of strength, the most
invigorating and at the same time most strengthening of all tonics.
In this respect there is nothing similar in the world.
And F. E. Bilz, in his book on natural healing methods, lets the poet
speak, who says: This immense expanse of water, the ocean, is the
condensed breath of God, and without it everything would be only a
cold and dry mass of rock — a breath which has bestowed fertility,
beauty, and life upon the Earth.
What role water plays in the household of the human body is evident
already from the fact that the latter consists of 90 percent water.
It is also known that humans and animals can much more easily do
without food for a long time than without water. The


---


- 219 -
average person, with complete abstinence from food and water, would
be able to live about three weeks. But if he takes in water, which
contains a certain measure of nutrients in substantive and energetic
form, he can hold out substantially longer. Dr. McNaughton reports
of a madman who was able to live for 53 days on water alone.
Modern civilized man today predominantly drinks bad water, has
therefore in many cases fallen out of the habit of drinking water,
and thereby inflicts serious harm on his body.
Dr. Munde writes: More recent investigations by Genth, Becquerel, and
others show that an increased intake of water into the body results
in an increased elimination of metabolic waste products, whereas a
decreased intake of water leads to a greater concentration of the
same and, in the urine, a larger quantity of uric acid — a fact to
which gout sufferers in particular may pay heed. As one can see from
a comparison of various medical experiments, there is for every
person a certain optimal quantity of water, which raises the
proportion of solid substances in the urine quite considerably.
In conclusion, reference should also be made to the fact that people
who drink much good and healthy water also always have a good
appetite and are therefore undoubtedly healthy.

Conclusion

If the foregoing expositions have been kept with a certain sharpness,
this was done only in the interest of the general public, because
with a continuation of today's methods the danger grows with every
day such that, if one does not intervene quickly and energetically,
chaotic conditions must arise within a very short time. There is
therefore no more time to lose.
That our water experts cannot be delighted by all these abuses,
uncovered with a certain ruthlessness, is understandable, but it
changes nothing about the existing fact. All the many attempts made
recently to withhold from the public the many clarifications
originating from me, by removing corresponding articles from books,
magazines, etc., are childish, since this only ensures all the more
that these clarifications reach the masses. Such attempts show only
a certain weakness and are in any case merely proof of the
irrefutability of what is said there. Likewise, the frequently made
objection that the methods used hitherto are practiced throughout
the world and therefore cannot be incorrect says nothing at all and
serves at most only to explain why things look so dreadful
throughout the whole world.


---


- 220 -
The best parry in this case can only be the ruthless attack,
whereby everyone has the right to defend and to refute at any time.
Whoever understands how to do it better in practice, or is able to
refute what is said here, should come forward. Many people will be in
a position to lend a working hand on the basis of their experience.
These, too, should come forward, for it is everyone's duty, in this
serious time, to stand by with advice and deed.
All those who wish to hold fast to the existing state of things out of
concern for their livelihood may consider that an existence built on
a false foundation, even if it appears secured by official
appointment, cannot be maintained in the long run, because an
impoverished people cannot pay taxes and can therefore not afford a
costly apparatus of officials.
Those who remain of the opinion that water is a lifeless substance,
to be mastered by mathematical formulas alone, may, when they fall
ill, summon to their sickbed a master of calculation instead of a
physician, so that the world is speedily delivered of thinkers of
such disposition.
The catchword "schematism," which already so greatly dominates our
working methods, has unfortunately also found more hearing among the
so-called thinkers than is good. Under the label "logical thinking"
or "mathematically trained thinking" there often hides today merely a
lack of thinking power, or laziness of thought. Yet the greatest
percentage of all discoveries and inventions has been made outside
those tracks laid out by the scientists, and has mostly filled them
with astonishment, indeed even with dismay.
Just as the overall progress of the world is ultimately caused by a
certain measure of discontent, and the characteristic phases within
it are always revolutions or wars, so too the great advances in the
intellectual realm have been brought about above all by revolutionary
thinkers.
In the empirical methods, as practiced at our water research
institutes, a certain shortsightedness has also taken hold. One still
clings crampedly to the outer appearance of the phenomenon and
neglects to study the far more important nature of the inner
processes. To be sure, among those responsible in this field there
are also some who have already recognized the slight value of these
merely superficial considerations, yet for the sake of the security
of their existence unfortunately leave everything as it has
customarily been.
The many grasping people, concerned only with their own well-being,
who are of the opinion that the blood of the Earth — the water —
and further the oils, the coal, and all other precious treasures can
be torn from the Earth with impunity, and that dirty trade can be
conducted not only in foodstuffs but even in the water destined for
poor and rich alike


---


- 221 -

can be driven, should be told that the despair of the great masses will put an end to these selfish aims sooner than they suspect.
All the others, but above all those who no longer have anything to lose, that is, first and foremost the youth, may help to give back to the homeland, first of all, its former forest and, with it, the healthy water to the Earth — for then we shall all be able to live and exist once more.
This year marked the hundredth birthday of the discoverer Nobel, who became a millionaire through his inventions, and who, a few years before his death, recognized the effects of his inventions and undoubtedly wished to make amends through his foundation for the terrible misfortune he had caused through his discovery. Since then, millions of human lives have fallen victim to this terrible instrument of destruction and war.
Further millions, indeed perhaps entire peoples and races, will still be robbed of their lives and their existence if humanity continues to employ only those inventions that are owed to mere chance and that lay bare only isolated secrets of the forces of nature. Without knowledge of the great interconnections, centuries will and must pass before the true face of a discovery initially regarded as a blessing can be recognized.
The example that people often must take detours that prove extremely disastrous for them before they recognize the true state of affairs is by no means unique.
If we study the article that appeared in the economic section of the "Deutsche Zeitung" No. 242 of 15 October 1933, we see that the German government is now setting out to correct a grave error that has likewise been committed for about a century, and that has perhaps caused even greater harm than the use of modern means of war. This article is entitled "New Forestry." Even though science has long recognized that everything practiced in modern forestry to date is wrong, and that since the introduction — around the middle of the last century — of the forest-science methods applied almost everywhere today, our forests have decayed qualitatively, what has been lacking until now was probably the necessary courage to admit the errors once committed. With the prohibition of clear-cutting, much has certainly been accomplished, but by no means everything. Here too, people merely want to gain time in order to save their own existence, or at least their retirement provision, at the expense of the general public. But this will not succeed; for this chapter in particular will be treated in such detail in later treatises that every schoolchild will see what is at stake here, and what terrible harm has been conjured upon all of humanity by the so-called modern science of forestry as a result of ignorance of the facts. The


---


- 222 -

The same applies to modern agriculture and to other modern achievements.
As for armaments and war preparations, it shall only be briefly mentioned here that in this field, too, the possibility exists of rendering every bomb- and gas-carrying aircraft, indeed even shells filled with poison and explosive, harmless with utmost ease through the simplest of measures. All other weapons of war will likewise become mere children's toys once humanity becomes clear about the forces that slumber in water.
And that everyone will learn to apply these forces, too, will be provided for. For if humanity wishes to ruin itself by force, then it should also be given the corresponding means into its hands, so that it can follow this desire as swiftly as possible.
What has been said above is bound to leave very mixed feelings in the reader. Among other things, one might arrive at the view that, in view of the many and undoubtedly correct observations and therefore valuable pointers, it would be entirely unnecessary to attack science and the whole of technology as fiercely as has been done here.
Unfortunately, however, this is absolutely necessary, because it would be completely pointless to seek contact in that direction.
As proof that a compromise, or an incorporation into the existing scientific edifice, of the discoveries only hinted at here and treated in detail later is not possible — because the error of today's science and the damage wrought by today's technology are too great — the following remarks may serve, which will be treated in great detail in further publications.
At the present rate of coal consumption of two billion tons per year, the point at which these important sources of energy will be exhausted has already drawn alarmingly near.
Within a few centuries, as science itself has calculated, the last oil reserves will have been extracted from the Earth, so that if we continue to manage our economy in this way, we shall have to look around for other sources of energy, since the loss of these energies would mean the destruction of present-day civilization.
Science is also making every serious effort to discover new energies, and believes it will achieve this, among other things, through the investigation of cosmic energies. This undertaking, however, displays not only a practically boundless one-sidedness, but also delivers the irrefutable proof of the untenability of previous scientific endeavors and goals, which — to use no harsher word — can only be described by the expression "utopias." A science that pursues such goals, however, can by no means be taken seriously, and least of all can it claim the right to intervene in a leading way in the destiny of humankind.


---


- 223 -

While the direction shown above is certainly still far from being the only correct one, it nevertheless already approaches the truth, which as such can only ever represent the relatively most useful error, because the investigation of the purest truth is an unattainable goal for human beings and will probably remain so.
The mere idea of turning to substitute energy sources only after the consumption of all reserves of coal, oil, or wood is so absurd that science as a whole has already condemned itself by this alone.
The temperatures prevailing in the interior of the Earth are the product of balancing processes that take place between the carbon substances contained within the Earth and the oxygen that enters the Earth with the water. If the last, highly organized carbon substances were ever to be torn out of the Earth, these balancing processes could no longer take place, and the Earth would have to cool down. Since it is practically impossible to actually remove all carbon substances from the Earth, these phenomena can only occur to the extent that the disturbances of the inner interconnections — caused by the extraction of carbon substances or by the aeration of the Earth — make themselves felt.
The effects of today's technical and economic interventions in the organism "Earth" must therefore, by natural law, lead to the following results:
If, through various external influences such as boreholes, the opening-up of the Earth through deep wells, shafts, and so on, through the excessive extraction of coal, metals, and the like, the balancing processes are suppressed and hence a cooling of the Earth's crust comes about, then in further consequence a cooling of the atmosphere must also occur. The same causes that bring about an excessive accumulation of oxygen in the atmosphere must also entail a concentration of oxygen as a result of the influence of cold.
In the course of time, the layers of air that normally stand under negative pressure will, for lack of upwelling groups of carbon substances, become heavier, sink down, and supersaturate both the vaporous and the liquid hydrosphere with oxygen.
If now water supersaturated with oxygen and therefore heavy reaches deeper layers of the geosphere — for example, the carbonsphere — the oxygen it carries will, at high temperatures, give rise to lively oxidation phenomena, which in their summation lead to local explosions, that is, to eruptions. In further consequence, the Earth's crust must burst, and with it a sudden upwelling of gaseous groups of carbon substances must occur. These relatively low-organized substances will only come into equilibrium with atmospheric oxygen at great altitudes and will trigger a regional division into various heat zones, whereby again more or


---


- 224 -

less violent air currents and sudden cold snaps will be caused.
In the equatorial regions, the rising of the carbon substances will be promoted by stronger heat-reflection phenomena.
The interactions that thereby occur may under certain circumstances become so great and so aggressive that the zone of equilibrium widens funnel-like in deeper regions; this leads to the general formation of so-called tornadoes and colossal whirlwind storms, which have been known in equatorial regions since time immemorial. Through these violent balancing processes, the water vapor is also forced into local accumulations, the consequences of which are the formation of severe thunderstorms and the occurrence of cloudbursts.
During strong eruptions, alongside large quantities of carbon substances, copious amounts of water vapor are also spewed out, which cause an increase in the resistance to the energy rays coming from the Sun and thus an increase in heat. The consequence of this phenomenon is a short-lived, luxuriant enrichment of vegetation — that is, an agricultural sham success — which, however, in turn leads to an increased consumption of gaseous carbon substances, which now can no longer be replenished from the interior of the Earth in the necessary and uniform measure. This in turn leads to a qualitative degradation of the forms of vegetation and to a depletion of the energy substances reflected back by the Sun — thus, in the final consequence, to a systematic cooling, that is, to the onset of a new ice age.
These developments are presently being brought about by the devastating activity of human beings in the fields of forestry, agriculture, water management, and energy management, because the prevailing one-sided views suppress the evenness of the water cycle and thereby also of the energy cycle, and the upwelling of the carbon substances. As already mentioned, the senseless working methods of human beings customary today must lead to a qualitative decline of the energy substances reflected back by the Sun, and ultimately — through the suppression of the oxidation processes in the atmosphere — to a reduced formation of heat, so that it can rightly be claimed that the next ice age is, as it were, being dragged in by the hair by today's science and technology.
For this reason, it was also a logical necessity that, with the advance of technology, the economic phenomena of decay known throughout the whole world should occur, and they will intensify at the same pace at which the sources of energy are extracted from the Earth to maintain technological progress. The greater the progress we achieve in technology, the deeper we shall and must sink economically and culturally. But that is not all! With the decline of the oxidation processes absolutely necessary within the Earth, enormous accumulations of water must first arise in the atmosphere and later in the Earth


---


- 225 -

occur of themselves, because the water can then be neither processed here nor there, that is to say, transformed.

The water that penetrates into the earth, or that rises up in the atmosphere and evaporates again — strongly oversaturated with oxygen and poor in carbon — will, for lack of the presence of its partner, the carbon substances, shift its freezing point, whereby the general climatic conditions must in due course change from the ground up. Furthermore, the single-pole charged groundwater, when it is relaxed (depressurized) by lack of carbon substances, must sink to depths where carbon substances may still be present, shift its boiling point there, oxidize prematurely, and trigger violent eruptions. All vegetation must, along with the sinking of the water, perish just as it once came, and the vegetation zone will, after monstrous catastrophes that must appear in the form of earthquakes, cloud-bursts and whirlstorms, etc., slowly but surely, according to law, freeze over.

The water catastrophes, already intensifying everywhere today, to which, as reported, for example in China an estimated 20 million human lives will fall victim, are for the time being still very harmless events compared with the catastrophes soon to be expected, which must inevitably occur if people continue to let themselves be guided and led by today's science.

For instance, the current explanation of the formation of rain is so incomplete that one cannot wonder enough how such a hypothesis could maintain itself for centuries.

Science explains the origin of rain through the condensation, occurring as a result of cold, of the water vapor contained in the air — an explanation that certainly comes close to the truth in the broader sense, but which in the end represents only a very incidental accompanying phenomenon, since, as extremely simple experiments prove, the formation of rain too can be traced back primarily only to the balancing processes described above, which can arise solely through the crossing of the upward-striving carbon substances and the oxygen groups descending with fine ice.

If the view of science were correct, then it would have to rain in the higher layers in winter and snow in summer, since, as is well known, the air temperatures, like the temperatures of the earth, undergo an opposite reversal with the change of the seasons.

In view of the consequential effects, here only very briefly hinted at, which must occur — purely according to law and therefore reliably — if today's scientific views continue to be pursued, only one viable path remains, and that is to make people suspicious or wary, for only in this way can, at the last hour, the impulse arise toward recognition of the actually existing and unimaginable danger, before which we misled human beings today stand, without


---


- 226 -

difference of races and nations, stand practically, perhaps still be triggered. Since, besides those who have led us all into this terrible dead end, and who with a downright heavenly unawareness would also lead us into chaos, there may yet be people who have preserved at least so much feeling of humanity as to not consciously bring our children into such appalling catastrophes, there exists at least still the possibility of tearing the blindfold from the eyes of the reasonable, in order to undertake, with the help of these few, an attempt at rescue, which our youth will undoubtedly support energetically, because it is, after all, a matter of their own future.

As is well known, there is no little herb against stupidity, and those who err unconsciously can hardly be called to account. But once the causes of the symptoms of decay showing themselves everywhere have been recognized, then any continuation of these working methods, which consciously lead the whole of humanity into ruin, would undoubtedly mean a crime.

Through the current technical and economic measures, it had to come — according to law and therefore reliably — to a systematic disturbance of the water balance, and thereby to an already very far-reaching suppression of the balancing processes that condition all life in nature.

The logical consequence of the suppression of the oxidation processes taking place between the building-up basic substances is an ever further spreading desolation and cooling of the all-nourishing vegetation zone.

With continuation of today's working principles, it must therefore — apart from the symptoms of decay and disease that assume ever greater dimensions — also inevitably come to a general world famine.

The insights gained from the foregoing exposition must compel us to face the following decision:

Either we renounce the achievements of today's science and technology, which bring only heavy damage, and put the currently prevailing intellectual leaders out in the cold — or we allow ourselves to be gradually put out in the cold by them, or, by all the rules of the art, in the truest sense of the word, be led onto the ice, in order to remain preserved for at least a future human race, in this strictly scientifically conserved form, as the warning end-product of a past "culture," for as long as can be imagined.

Vienna, November 1933.

Issued in July 1934

AUSTRIAN PATENT OFFICE

PATENT SPECIFICATION No. 138296

Viktor Schauberger in Vienna
Water Conduction in Pipes and Channels — Additional Patent

Filed on 2 November 1932
Start of patent term: 15 March 1934


---


The invention concerns a further elaboration of the water conduction in pipes and channels according to Patent No. 134543, with built-in, guide-vane-like surfaces projecting from the wall toward the center, each of which is curved in such a way that it urges the water from the wall toward the middle of the water conduit, whereby, according to the parent patent, the essence of the invention in the case of pipes consists in the fact that the guide-vane-like surfaces are arranged in the course of a multiple-start helical twist (drall). A particular embodiment of the guide-vane-like surfaces consists, according to Patent No. 134543, in that they are provided with grooves running in a twist-like manner in the direction of flow of the water.

The invention now concerns a further embodiment of the guide-vane-like surfaces, whose purpose is to favor the advancing movement of the water in the core relative to the water conduction in the marginal zones.

Simple inhibition of the marginal zones would lead to turbulence phenomena in the boundary layer between the core zone and the marginal zone, and would adversely affect the formation of a well-developed core zone. The invention therefore aims to decompose the marginal zone into individual vortex structures which, on account of their inner stability, are, so to speak, solid structures with little tendency to dissolve, and which in their entirety yield a water mantle that favors the advancing of the water core. The elements are wound in the manner of a torsion spring, so that two guide-surface elements, fundamentally configured as in Fig. 1, are formed. These two surface elements have the task of imparting to the water threads of the marginal zone of the water stream arriving in the twist direction (3) a screw-shaped movement, so that a screw-shaped sub-movement arises within the screw movement of the entire water mantle. In the drawing, the subject of the invention is shown in an exemplary embodiment in Fig. 1 in top view, and in Fig. 2, seen obliquely from the front, against the direction of flow. Fig. 3 shows the

- 229 -

surface unwrapped (developed).

In the pipe (1), the guide-vane-shaped elements (2) are arranged, as in the parent patent, in the course of a multiple-start twist (3). On leaving the vane part (5), the water threads are each always imparted a movement directed toward the middle of the cross-section. The guiding of the water is favored by grooves (6), and since the grooves converge conically, a press-water formation is triggered, which is likewise intended to urge the rushing-through conveyed material toward the center.

The guide surfaces may also be composed of individual elements separated from one another.

Patent claims:


---


1. Water conduit according to Patent No. 134543, with guide-vane-like surfaces projecting from the wall toward the center, characterized in that these surfaces are wound in the manner of a torsion spring such that in each case two cooperating vane-shaped elements arise, of which the one separates the marginal zone of the flow from the core zone, and the element following in the direction of flow additionally imparts a circulating movement to the bundle of separated stream threads, which moves in a screw path on account of the screw-shaped arrangement of the guide-vane-like surfaces, whereby the marginal zone is decomposed into individual stable vortex structures.

2. Water conduit according to claim 1, characterized in that the guide-vane-like surfaces have the form of approximately rhomboid strips, whose diagonally opposite obtuse-angled corners are bent up toward the same side of the surface.

..and there I had an experience for the first time that — decisively influenced my whole destiny. And indeed: I wanted to jump over the brook — wanted to jump across the brook and sought a hold with the mountain staff — as in pole vaulting, so that I wouldn't slip — and in that moment I see that a strong trout darts away. It was colored in such a way that one could barely see it, like a stone in the rushing water. At first I didn't know what was happening — it had darted off. Then I saw: there stand the strong trout, strung like organ pipes — motionless in this rushing, clear mountain water, spring water.

Below, roughly a kilometer away, the water plunges 60 meters, and above was the spring. And there I said to myself: how do trout even get up here. There is the waterfall — no question of an artificial fish hatchery — there is the spring. At this altitude, 1000 meters, no worm, no food, no fly, the snow lay everywhere — right? There were these chamois in a cirque, always snow — cold, even in summer, because it was an enclosed cirque, so little sun came in.

The two questions: Where do the trout come from,
and why can these trout, in defiance of all laws of gravity, stand so motionless?
That left me no more sleep.
